> **Entry:** (`gate:phase5:exit`) and accepted STATE-constructor decision
> **Packages:** tapscript, linker, transaction, vectors
> **Operation:** `announce-maturity`

## Goal · `sec:phase6:goal`

Integrate authenticated metadata-dependent STATE succession in the smallest
STATE-changing operation.

The implementation must preserve STATE metadata and static code continuity
under the accepted constructor design.

## Deliverables · `sec:phase6:deliverables`

### STATE constructor

Implement the accepted constructor recipe:

- canonical STATE metadata schema;
- deterministic metadata commitment;
- linked static operation-program identity;
- predecessor constructor authentication;
- successor constructor reconstruction;
- internal-key policy;
- metadata path unspendability;
- target control-path recipe;
- constructor resource formula.

### Operation relation

Implement `announce-maturity`:

- canonical STATE predecessor;
- maturity currently unannounced;
- announced cycle within minimum and maximum leads;
- successor maturity announced;
- all economic state fields unchanged;
- operator authorization;
- no RESV use;
- optional isolated sponsor flow;
- STATE succession projection.

### ABI

Define:

- STATE input slot;
- STATE successor slot;
- operator witness role;
- constructor/static-root metadata;
- sponsor region;
- target transaction version/sequence constraints.

## Required vectors · `sec:phase6:vectors`

Positive:

- minimum valid lead;
- maximum valid lead;
- valid operator;
- sponsorless/sponsored;
- exact successor metadata.

Negative:

- below minimum;
- above maximum;
- second announcement;
- sealed predecessor;
- wrong operator;
- wrong STATE asset or amount;
- wrong predecessor metadata;
- changed unaffected state field;
- wrong successor metadata;
- successor under another static root;
- extra/missing operation leaf;
- wrong internal key;
- wrong parity/control path;
- metadata-leaf spend;
- unexpected RESV;
- missing STATE successor;
- stale constructor from another bundle.

## History evidence · `sec:phase6:history`

Extend constructor and root-history fixtures to reject:

- intermediate constructor substitution;
- final cursor restored after an invalid intermediate STATE edge;
- old-bundle predecessor/new-bundle successor without an approved migration;
- post-sealing STATE revival.

## Resource evidence · `sec:phase6:resources`

Measure complete announce-maturity transactions with:

- representative and maximum metadata;
- deepest control path;
- operator signature;
- maximum sponsor candidate;
- constructor verification.

Success here validates the constructor mechanism for this operation, not
resource feasibility of every later STATE operation.

## Wave-0 ground truth · `sec:phase6:ground-truth`

Guide 14 §3.1 asks Wave 0 to record the existing owners of the STATE semantics. The Wave-0 rows recorded the tenth-review disposition and the archival arithmetic but not this census, so it is recorded here from the tree as it stands at the Wave-1 opening, each entry naming its owner file and line. Every line below was read at that tip before it was written; where a cited line had moved, the line here is the one the tree holds.

### Wave-1 rulings · `rule:phase6:wave1-rulings`

1. The realization crate gains a `Cycle` domain. Its semantic-domains section already anticipates exactly this for later operations ((`sec:realization:domains`)), so the domain is an expected extension rather than a new kind of fact in that crate. The same section fixes the classification: a count is not an amount merely because both are integers, and a cycle is neither — it is an ordinal, and only comparison and checked offset are meaningful on it.

2. The pipeline-facing typed STATE metadata — `Maturity`, `StateMetadata`, `AnnouncementLeadBounds`, the total `announce_maturity` transition, and later its canonical codec — lives in the realization crate. The library direction runs architecture, realization, compiler, target-elements, tapscript, linker, transaction (§3.4), and no pipeline crate names the model: `packages/realization/Cargo.toml:16-18` names `architecture`, `petgraph` and `thiserror` only, and the compiler, tapscript, linker and transaction manifests name no model either. A type owned by the model would therefore be unreachable to the constructor and ABI waves, which is the decisive constraint. The projection law against the model's `PoolState` — lossless round trip, field-for-field agreement, and agreement of `announce_maturity` with the model branch on every refusal — is proved in the model's conformance tests, which see both crates because `packages/model/Cargo.toml:19` and `:23` name `architecture` and `realization`. That test is the drift tripwire: it is what keeps one owner per fact while the type sits in the crate the pipeline can reach.

3. Lead bounds enter the transition as a typed parameter carrying the model's validity law — the minimum at least one, and the minimum not above the maximum — so an invalid bound pair cannot be constructed and the transition need not re-check it. No architecture bound is minted in Wave 1. `BoundId` is a published discriminant set whose header forbids reordering or renumbering after publication and admits new variants only under a new architecture schema version (`packages/architecture/src/ids.rs:1-5`), so naming a maturity-lead bound there is an architecture schema change, not a realization change, and Wave 1 is not the place to make one.

4. Refusals are distinct closed sums per layer, spelled as §6.6 spells them: a transition sum, and, when the codec bite lands, a separate decode sum. A decoder that could return a semantic refusal, or a transition that could return a byte-level one, would make each layer a partial owner of the other's vocabulary and leave the composed precedence undefined. The model keeps its `Guard` names and maps onto the transition sum in conformance, which is the same discharge shape `G14C-06` states for Waves 1 and 4.

5. The canonical metadata encoding is big-endian, with a `u32` representation nonce. Big-endian is the first-party precedent already load-bearing in the model's own codec (`packages/model/src/ledger.rs:1480`), and matching it means one byte-order convention to reason about across first-party encodings rather than two. The little-endian prototype in the conformance package is not counter-evidence: it is unpromoted evidence code whose own header refuses ABI standing. The encoding is an ABI-identity component — D006 binds constructor and metadata schemas and representation requirements into ABI identity ((`rule:abi:identity`)) — so it is recorded here, before any bytes exist, rather than left to be discovered from whatever the first implementation happened to write.

6. Operator authorization is an operation-level evidence-required relation and an operator constructibility class. The existing owner/permissionless bodies and public-permissionless/owners-of classes cannot state it (`packages/realization/src/relation.rs:20-25`, `:42-68`): STATE has no owner, permissionlessness requires no signer, and the model requires the operator (`packages/realization/src/evaluate.rs:371-374`, `:828-840`; `packages/model/src/ops/maturity.rs:34-40`). Because the observation has presented signers but no expected operator identity (`packages/realization/src/observation.rs:210-220`), `OperatorAuthorization` yields `EvidenceRequired` like substrate conservation (`packages/realization/src/evaluate.rs:291-304`), never a forged pass. `ConstructibilityClass::Operator` reuses the graph's existing operator authorization, availability discharge, derived announce-maturity case, and test (`packages/realization/src/constructibility.rs:34-43`, `:276-289`; `packages/realization/src/validate.rs:493`; `packages/realization/src/tests/constructibility_tests.rs:400-410`). Wave 3 retains operator-key and target-signature closure (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:2925-2935`).

7. Each of `omega`, `y_l`, `y_t`, `q`, `cycle`, and `maturity` is a field-level public fact, keyed separately for STATE input and output. `FactId` has no STATE key and `FamilyAmount` is aggregate family value (`packages/realization/src/identity.rs:16-77`), so neither can name the six public fields in `StateMetadata` (`packages/realization/src/state.rs:44-70`). Add typed field identities, not an opaque blob or caller-authored truth; D005 requires explicit encoding or a public commitment with authenticated public opening (`plans/decisions/005-value-representation.md:84-97`).

8. STATE admits exactly `Explicit` and `PublicCommitted`: they are D005's two public carriers (`plans/decisions/005-value-representation.md:84-97`) and the existing compact-ASH `Relation::Representation` pair (`packages/realization/src/declarations/compact_ash.rs:337-344`). `PrivateCommitted` cannot carry ruling 7's public facts.

9. STATE has exactly six lifecycle exits — `AdmitDeposits`, `Cycle`, `Redeem`, `ReceiptRelabel`, `Clear`, and `AnnounceMaturity` — matching its six `ObjectSpec` mutators (`packages/architecture/src/spec.rs:2047-2063`). Declare all six under both ruled representations, and scope-gate a content pin comparing that census with the scoped State object's mutators: the generic weld derives expectations from declarations and cannot detect coherent omission from both relation and graph (`packages/realization/src/validate.rs:525-551`, `:635-678`). The announcement mirror separately checks its exact-one STATE input and output (`packages/architecture/src/spec.rs:1607-1638`) using `StateInput` and `StateOutput`; generic validation retains cardinality and recognition (`packages/realization/src/validate.rs:318-375`).

10. `ConformanceReport::is_conformant` already admits `RelationStatus::EvidenceRequired` beside `Passed` and `StaticallyValidated` because external proof incompleteness is not a semantic failure (`packages/realization/src/evaluate.rs`, the `impl ConformanceReport` block). The dependency walk in `evaluate_operation` now agrees: only `Failed` and `Blocked` prerequisites block, while `EvidenceRequired` releases its dependents as an established external premise. The distinction is observable through the announcement's `AuthorizationBeforeClosure` and `AuthorizationBeforeConstructibility` edges (`packages/realization/src/declarations/announce_maturity.rs`): without that release, no announcement observation could produce a conformant runtime report.

### Wave-2 rulings · `rule:phase6:wave2-rulings`

11. The announcement lead bounds become architecture-owned bound identifiers in the public `BoundId` census in `packages/architecture/src/ids.rs`, beside `FeeSponsorInputMax`. Question (b) already excludes unkeyed constants from a window expression; the announce-maturity architecture row already publishes `FeeSponsorInputMax` as the runtime bound it uses (`packages/architecture/src/spec.rs`); and the compiler's `RuntimeBound` source kind already carries an architecture `BoundId` (`packages/compiler/src/source.rs`). Independent validation of the announcement window therefore needs one public owner, while public recovery does not consume the bounds (`sec:phase6:carrier-proof`). Adding the two identifiers requires an architecture schema bump and a semantic-hash move under (`[ADR021-rule:identity:recipe-permanence]`), isolated as `T11-021` before B5: the realization's `AnnouncementLead` fact keys then resolve to those bounds, model conformance proves the architecture values agree with `min_maturity_lead` and `max_maturity_lead` in `packages/model/src/constants.rs`, and `T11-017` can state the altered-lead-bounds corruption.

12. Operator authorization stays target-side evidence. `OperationObservation` carries presented protocol and sponsor signers but no expected operator identity (`packages/realization/src/observation.rs`), the operator relations remain `EvidenceRequired` in `packages/realization/src/evaluate.rs`, and the compiler's external classification in `packages/compiler/src/proof.rs` stands. Wave 3 discharges that evidence through operator-key encoding and target-native signature control, with the model's signer check in `packages/model/src/ops/maturity.rs` remaining the conformance ground truth. The shape deliberately abstracts the operator's identity out of analysis: the plan states the obligation and the capability that discharges it, while the deployment profile binds who satisfies it. That gives each fact one verdict owner and keeps the operator identity as deployment data rather than architecture data; the ADR-021 identity chain places keys and key policies at the deployment-profile end (`[ADR021-rem:identity:chain]`), so architecture ownership would make every key rotation a schema bump and invert the chain by making architecture depend on a per-deployment fact. The visible price is an honestly conformant but evidence-incomplete report until the deployment layer discharges the obligation (`packages/realization/src/evaluate.rs`).

13. Public STATE fields become side-indexed facts: `FactId::StateField` is keyed by operation, transaction side, and field, reusing the `TransactionSide` type that `FamilyCount`, `FamilyAmount`, and `FamilyRecognized` already carry (`packages/realization/src/identity.rs`). That removes the only family-like exception from a typed vocabulary: the declaration census makes the uniform shape directly checkable, just as the separate hash-citation audit checks its own complete vocabulary (`packages/realization/src/declarations/announce_maturity.rs`, `packages/labels/src/hashcite.rs`). The more general shape is required because STATE's six lifecycle exits — `AdmitDeposits`, `Cycle`, `Redeem`, `ReceiptRelabel`, `Clear`, and `AnnounceMaturity` — all emit successors under laws that range from adding admitted principal to pending entitlement, through redemption's redeemed-amount and floored-payout subtractions and clear's clamped ASH subtraction, to cycle's split and zeroing, while relabel copies STATE and announcement changes maturity (`packages/model/src/ops/`); the compiler's current predecessor key plus two-case per-field effect can state only the announcement case, whereas a relation needs the output key as its subject to state every successor law (`packages/compiler/src/maturity_announcement_requirements.rs`). The successor leaf is public in the accepted transaction's output set (`packages/compiler/src/maturity_announcement_requirements.rs`), and an output-side fact is an observation checked by one stated law, not a second verdict, so ruling 12's one-verdict-owner shape remains intact. Each field's output-side fact is related to input-side facts and any other public operands by a stated symbolic law in the requirement layer, and the realization executes that law; both sides carry `InitialVisibility::Public` because initial visibility is the disclosure graph's confidentiality baseline rather than a claim about creation order (`packages/realization/src/declassification.rs`). This follows the existing public compact-ASH input/output amount declarations and contrasts with transfer-live's private input/output amounts (`packages/realization/src/declarations/compact_ash.rs`, `packages/realization/src/declarations/transfer_live.rs`), while acceptance-time publication remains a recovery, target-evidence, and root-history obligation (`packages/compiler/src/maturity_announcement_requirements.rs`). An exit declares only the sides it has, but all six STATE exits have an output: STATE's architecture object has no deallocator (`packages/architecture/src/spec.rs`), and sealing redemption emits the sealed STATE tombstone (`docs/attestation/realization.md`). The successor is named only by its output keys, retiring the requirement layer's symbolic successor roles in favour of input key, output key, and law (`packages/compiler/src/maturity_announcement_requirements.rs`). The executable declaration census remains 26 relations and 23 edges because disclosure nodes are a separate graph (`packages/realization/src/declassification.rs`, `packages/realization/src/tests/announce_maturity_tests.rs`); lowering the laws into executable relations is a later bite. `T11-021` therefore combines this side-indexed refit with ruling 11's architecture-owned lead bounds and lands before B4 (`T11-016`).

14. Ruling 2 stands: `StateMetadata` is a realization-owned type whose projection from the model's canonical pool state is proved by model conformance (`packages/realization/src/state.rs`, `packages/model/src/tests/state_metadata_conformance_tests.rs`); the dependency direction admits no other placement that the constructor and ABI waves can reach, while admitting the model crate into the pipeline would make the conformance oracle a build input and would raise the dependency-admission record of the backlog's §8.2 at (`rule:backlog:dependency-entry`), whose eight conditions are written for third-party dependencies, so whether a first-party workspace crate needs that record is stated nowhere.

15. The architecture data census names cycle and maturity, so the field-slicing law of `G14C-14` has an architecture identifier for every STATE field and the realization's six field facts all resolve to public identifiers through `StateField::data_id()` (`packages/architecture/src/ids.rs`, `packages/realization/src/identity.rs`); those names rode the same architecture schema bump as the lead bounds, so one identity movement carried both.

#### Ownership shapes · `rule:phase6:ownership-shapes`

A consensus value that a plan must validate against is a schema-declared symbol with exactly one published definition, versioned with the schema and referenced by identifier from every plan, never a literal in a check and never an unnamed runtime struct. A verdict that needs key material or an identity is an external obligation emitted by the analysis together with the capability that discharges it, discharged where the key lives and bound by the deployment profile, never a comparison against an identity handed to the analysis. A value the transition itself produces is a public fact on the output side, related to its inputs by a stated law, never a second name for the same quantity and never a private surrogate for something not yet created. The analysis therefore never names an identity, and a report that is conformant but evidence-incomplete is the honest state until the deployment layer runs (`[ADR021-rem:identity:chain]`).

### Wave-3 rulings · `rule:phase6:wave3-rulings`

16. A maturity-announcement plan carries the fact that an operator key is needed and the responsibilities its signature owns, while the operator profile is a source-selected, revision-bound tapscript establishment type mirroring the established owner pattern, as landed in `0.6.160-dev`, and the key value is deployment data bound in a candidate-only linker record, as landed in `0.6.161-dev`; therefore no operator or profile field belongs on a semantic request and no release-profile serialization is warranted until a release consumer exists, while a profile presented by a request or returned by an executor is provenance rather than evidence because the established ownership shape keeps semantic analysis free of deployment identity and requires reviewed source, recomputation, native acceptance and protected-term controls (`packages/architecture/src/deployment.rs:68,175–191`; `packages/linker/src/live_deployment.rs:50–88`; `packages/tapscript/src/authorization.rs:471–496,694–805`; `plans/phases/06-state-and-maturity.md:166,176`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:958–975,1393–1402`).

17. The public test signer's material is a closed set of fixed handles resolved only inside the conformance layer and the Python executor from the disposable scalars committed in the vectors crate, with the first and third scalars serving the positive and wrong-key controls; the scalar never appears on the wire, in command-line arguments or in configuration, and that API prohibits key files, wallet seeds, production credentials and arbitrary digest signing, keeps the NUMS constructor key separate, and states an interface restriction rather than a proof about misuse elsewhere because the constructor and disposable-material rules explicitly bound what the repository claims (`packages/vectors/src/live_plan.rs:57–83`; `packages/target-elements-conformance/src/test_material.rs:137–171`; `packages/target-elements-conformance/src/constructor/internal_key.rs:5–24`; `scripts/elements-native-executor.py:641–647`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:532–550,1373–1380,3679–3698`).

18. Strict response acceptance requires exactly one result for one outstanding case and its exact input, a fixed signer and closed profile, a byte-identical finalized echo, one 64-byte Schnorr witness item under the default type, and a matching leaf, control and deployment census, with no success artifact on refusal and typed rejection of every missing, duplicate, unexpected, alternate, malformed, mismatched or post-finalization mutation case; native acceptance and protected-term controls remain additional evidence because every binding must be checked before witness insertion and an echo alone cannot establish what the target accepted (`packages/target-elements-conformance/src/protocol.rs:2784–2845,3073–3083`; `packages/transaction/src/live_census.rs:1114–1205,1473–1492`; `packages/transaction/src/live_signing.rs:268–330`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:1382–1389,2094–2128`).

19. The native protocol gains the guide's target-generic script-path signing capability with its own subject and result, and Rust and Python move together from revision 7 to revision 8 while capability negotiation remains in force; the request binds the finalized bytes, input index, complete ordered spent outputs, leaf and control data, fixed signer and profile, and session genesis, while revision-7 parsing and records remain preserved because a new signing record changes the strict wire schema but does not erase the historical contract (`packages/target-elements-conformance/src/protocol.rs:218,437–450,2191–2201`; `packages/transaction/src/live_message.rs:283–315`; `scripts/elements-native-executor.py:4509–4533,510`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:1351–1371,1391,3675–3677`; `packages/vectors/src/live_corpus_native_v2_r7.rs:1533`).

20. The affine right is a registry scoped by deployment, branch or checkpoint, predecessor version, and selected operator and profile, holding the exact candidate bytes, where issuance consumes the right, an identical retry returns the cached result without a second signing action, replacement is pre-sign only, a post-dispatch timeout is indeterminate and cannot reopen the right, and reorganization requires newly evidenced context; its log claims nothing about signatures outside the registry because the branch-indexed model and finalization boundary support only that scoped evidence, and the conceptual register's adoption of its equivocation candidate is a separate step that waits on the native evidence of `T11-031` and is recorded by `T11-032` (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:1986–2019`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen_conceptual_register.md:23–43,57–61,85`; `packages/transaction/src/live_handoff.rs:200–203`; `packages/target-elements-conformance/src/executor.rs:2050–2118`).

21. Wave 3 constructs one minimal finalized synthetic script-path candidate with a committed approved operator key and exact protected-byte controls, while the STATE constructor and ABI remain with Waves 4 and 7, the generic `Incomplete` capability assessments remain unchanged, selected-profile evidence stays the narrower witness, and metadata and nonce binding through commitments remains an obligation until the real constructor exists because this wave proves operator-signing closure without promoting unestablished generic capability or leaking a synthetic schema into STATE (`packages/target-elements/src/capability.rs:723–737`; `packages/target-elements/src/authorization.rs:797–925`; `packages/transaction/src/live_message.rs:272–318`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:2941–2944,2952–2980,3040–3068`).

### Wave-4 rulings · `rule:phase6:wave4-rulings`

22. `CandidateStateConstructor` and its closed refusal sum live in `packages/tapscript` as a new module, consuming realization's `EncodedStateMetadata` through a newly admitted production dependency and the workspace's existing `sha2` for tagged hashes, taking curve arithmetic through a capability trait that downstream code supplies, while the conformance prototype stays an independent oracle and no helper crate is created; because the dependency direction places tapscript downstream of realization and upstream of the linker, §9.7 requires the linker to consume every constructor dependency as a typed reference, which is expressible only with the constructor upstream of the linker, the guide's suggested subject names tapscript, and the manifest's stated reason for excluding realization concerns an assessment reading architecture-owned facts past the compiler's projection, which does not apply to a constructor that must commit the canonical bytes only realization's codec produces; the linker was considered and declined as host because its tree code is bundle-symbol-specific and the constructor is the program identity a STATE output commits to; the change carries the backlog's dependency-admission record (`packages/tapscript/Cargo.toml:15-46`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:761-865,1406-1428,1511-1528,2977-2980`; `packages/realization/src/state_codec.rs:18-63`).

23. The always-aborting metadata program is promoted in its proven three-instruction form — a push of the exact canonical metadata bytes, a push of the empty item, and `Verify` — expressed as typed instructions with a full pattern record that declares no success state and no non-aborting failure, an abort for every admitted initial stack, the exact metadata as its protected source, a non-executing witness role, its resource figures, its native evidence status and its residuals; because the pushed bytes are what the leaf hash covers, the empty push and `Verify` end evaluation before anything else can execute, an empty program is refused since an inherited true item would satisfy it, and the abstract oracle already proves the form's success and non-aborting sets empty (`packages/target-elements-conformance/src/constructor/metadata_leaf.rs:47-79`; `packages/target-elements-conformance/src/tests/constructor_tests.rs:588-659`; `packages/tapscript/src/stack.rs:157-229`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:1443-1466,1558-1578`).

24. Wave 4 defines the closed `StateStaticSubtree` type with its empty, incomplete, duplicate and conflicting refusals and makes the constructor a function of a typed subtree, exercising derivation and the deterministic-derivation exit over test-supplied leaves that satisfy the type's roles; the production static subtree is Wave 5's product, and Wave 5's first bite re-runs the Wave-4 derivation and golden tests over the real announcement leaves, which the Wave-4 record states explicitly rather than claiming an exact production root; because §9.4 forbids a placeholder inside the constructor's production input and §5.2 fixes the subtree as exact, while a typed test leaf under an announcement role is a test fixture and not a placeholder the constructor carries, and deterministic derivation is a property of the function that test leaves establish (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:892-899,1467-1479,2952-3012`).

25. The representation-nonce search runs candidates 0 through 4,095 — an attempt budget of 4,096, the prototype matrix's measured figure — refuses a zero budget as `ResourceBoundExceeded`, retries only on an above-order tweak, an identity result or a failed branch-side relation, records every rejected lower candidate and the selected nonce as host-leastness evidence, and reports exhaustion as `RepresentationSearchExhausted` with the later-admissible-nonce residual stated; the nonce stays a big-endian `u32`; because the guide fixes the scan from zero to a symbolic bound with the branch-side relation inside the retry condition, `R-1 NONCE` makes leastness host policy carried as evidence rather than a target claim, and the only measured budget in the tree is 4,096; the budget is a policy constant open to revision on evidence (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:900-946`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen_conceptual_register.md:45-49,87`; `packages/target-elements-conformance/src/census/constructor.rs:76-81`; `packages/realization/src/state_codec.rs:27-53`).

26. The constructor reuses the exact prototype NUMS x-coordinate, admitted only after Wave 4 independently recomputes it as SHA-256 over the uncompressed generator and lifts it onto the curve, recording that recomputation as the §5.5 confirmation that Wave 0 did not record, stating the discrete-log and preimage residual, and treating `InternalKeyNotAPoint` as input validation rather than evidence about key-path knowledge; the exit reads that no key-path escape is claimed, not that none exists; because §5.5 admits the standard key only after its assumptions are confirmed and forbids every other key class, the derivation is reproducible from public constants, and a refused key-path attempt establishes only its own refusal (`packages/target-elements-conformance/src/constructor/internal_key.rs:20-58`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:948-957`; `plans/backlog.md:1234-1241`).

27. `StateThreadAnchor` is defined in `packages/transaction/src/operator_right.rs` beside `BranchContext` and `RightScope`, with private fields and fallible construction carrying the six `G14C-04` categories — branch and network context, starting outpoint, asset or issuance provenance, constructor generation, checkpoint policy and continuity evidence — plus explicit synthetic-origin and evidence-status fields; Wave 4 states and tests only the conditional theorem that one uniquely anchored predecessor has at most one accepted continuation per selected branch, Waves 9 and 10 attach accepted continuity and history evidence, and `R-3 ANCHOR` is adopted on the pointer to the constructor and anchor tests and never on the synthetic origin; because realization may name no outpoint, network or deployment, the linker carries no transaction outpoints, and transaction already owns the branch-indexed scope vocabulary the anchor extends (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen_conceptual_register.md:51-55,88`; `packages/transaction/src/operator_right.rs:21-118`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:977-995`).

28. Wave 4 emits typed STATE reference declarations from the constructor and a constructor-local deterministic graph with its strongly connected components, sufficient to name any unresolved cycle as `ConstructorReferenceCycleUnresolved` while accepting no cut; Wave 6 owns binding-time resolution, authenticated cuts, the residual-DAG proof, canonical ordering and linker integration; the handoff artifact is the constructor's typed declaration list, consumed by a linker module that freezes canonical nodes and edges; because §9.7 assigns graph and component computation to both waves without naming the artifact, `G14C-08` assigns cut removal and residual acyclicity to Wave 6, and the existing graph is bundle-symbol-specific (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:1511-1528`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen_conceptual_register.md:92-93`; `packages/linker/src/graph.rs:35-53,254-378,411-537`).

29. `StateLeafRole` distinguishes the metadata commitment from each static announcement or support role, and `StateControlRecipe` carries typed reconstruction inputs — role, leaf version, output parity, exact internal key, executing leaf hash and ordered sibling hashes — and derives the control bytes rather than storing them; in the depth-one tree each direct leaf has one sibling and a 65-byte control block, a static leaf's path is its internal path followed by the metadata leaf hash, and `ControlPathTooDeep` arises when an inner static path of 128 nodes gains the outer sibling or a deployment limit is exceeded; because bytes alone can prove neither role and path uniqueness nor regenerate after a parity change (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:1429-1442`; `packages/target-elements-conformance/src/constructor/tree.rs:38-63,109-130,459-490`; `packages/target-elements/src/opcode.rs:46-90`).

30. The constructor constructs and exposes the twelve typed field-to-encoded-slice commitments — the input and output sides over the six `StateField` members — and tests them against the realization transition without marking any `FactId` observed or accepted; Wave 7 populates observation and ABI carriage and the evaluation layer decides the facts from that evidence; because the facts are defined as observed values of a primitive carrier that does not exist before the ABI, while the commitments are a property of the constructor's bytes (`packages/realization/src/identity.rs:16-69,111-134`; `packages/realization/src/evaluate.rs:516-566`; `packages/vectors/src/maturity_operator.rs:154-191`).

31. The guide governs where the closure record's handoff of finding 21 to Wave 4 overreaches: Wave 4 adds only the target-independent decision vocabulary and a fail-closed evaluation seam in realization that keeps the relation external until a witness is supplied, Wave 5 produces the authenticated membership witness with its operator-authorization pattern, and Wave 7 binds it to finalized observation bytes; the closure record's handoff sentence is corrected to that division; because the Wave-4 list has no evaluation deliverable, the pattern census assigns operator authentication to Wave 5, and a decision over an observed authorization needs the observation only the ABI binds (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:2952-3012`; `packages/realization/src/evaluate.rs:301-319,428-433`).

32. The two target-native rows of §9.3's evidence census — no accepted spend through the metadata leaf, and every attempted spend rejected — are recorded as outstanding with Wave 8's canonical safety evidence as their owner, and Wave 4 makes the production leaf and its fixture schema ready for that run; Wave 4's exit that the metadata leaf has no success path is met on the abstract census alone and the card says so, so no reader counts the native rows answered; because the Wave-4 deliverable names abstract and oracle tests, Wave 8 owns target controls and the safety matrix, and evidence classes remain separate; the alternative reading, which makes the native rows a Wave-4 exit, would need a separately scoped native carrier and is recorded as declined (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:414-455,1443-1466,2952-2980,3070-3095`).

33. The shared capture writer retains the exact funding request bytes now, as a Wave-4 bite with node-free round-trip and census tests, because lossless evidence plumbing has no reason to wait; a constructor-specific ceremony and Rust test identity are minted only with an actual constructor carrier and are recorded as a Wave-8 dependency, because a ceremony census entry without a carrier would be an empty identity (`packages/vectors/tests/common/mod.rs:350-401,1042-1080`; `packages/vectors/src/maturity_operator.rs:582-603`).

### Wave-5 rulings · `rule:phase6:wave5-rulings`

The Wave-5 opening study's five decisions and one further decision on the shape of the first code bites are ruled here on the merits and open to revision at the candidate audit like every ruling on this card.

34. The production static subtree of the candidate STATE output is exactly one leaf, the composed maturity-announcement program under `StateLeafRole::Announcement`, and no `Support(n)` leaf is admitted unless a complete component recipe names a consumer that the announcement program cannot carry, in which case exactly the leaves that consumer needs are added with deterministic identities and order; a second announcement leaf stays refused by the one-role rule the constructor already enforces; because the control recipes, the resource projection and the golden evidence all scale with the leaf set, and an unconsumed leaf is the placeholder inside production input that §9.4 forbids (`packages/tapscript/src/state_constructor.rs:335-496,1031-1041`; `packages/tapscript/src/tests/state_constructor_tests.rs:76-172`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:892-899,1467-1479`).

35. The authenticated membership-witness producer lives in `packages/transaction` beside operator signing: it consumes the deployment binding, the revision-checked established operator profile, the verified authorization result and the affine right, states the mapping from the deployment's verified operator to the model's abstract operator explicitly, and emits realization's `ObservedOperatorMembership` with a provenance text naming itself and its run; transaction therefore names realization's witness type directly, admitted in the transaction package card on the direction realization to tapscript to linker to transaction that already holds in the dependency closure, without touching the card's parallel-semantic-table clause since no formula is evaluated; the association of the decision with finalized observation bytes stays with Wave 7 as ruling 31 fixes; because the transaction boundary is the only place that verifies the frozen message and the deployment, a leaf verifier in tapscript must not become an authority over deployment identity, and vectors is evidence code (`packages/realization/src/evaluate.rs:106-170`; `packages/tapscript/src/operator_authorization.rs:479-575`; `packages/linker/src/operator_deployment.rs:124-230`; `packages/transaction/src/operator_signing.rs:425-531`; `packages/transaction/src/operator_right.rs:296-391`).

36. Wave 5's pattern records are STATE-specific: a record type in tapscript patterned after `LiveTransferPattern` carries every §10 field — identity, semantic owner, prerequisites, typed fragment, the abstract success, failure and abort contract, source requirements, witness role, constructibility, disclosure, resource projection, target evidence and residuals — with the landed `StateMetadataPattern` adapted or embedded as the metadata-commitment component, every composed record's sources, evidence, disclosure and residuals derived by union over one component recipe, pattern identities minted only after each complete record is abstractly walked, the compact `BackendPattern` left unwidened, and no cross-operation generalization attempted this wave; because the compact identities and owners are compact-ASH-specific and lack the §10 fields, and a general record designed after one STATE operation would be designed on one example (`packages/tapscript/src/pattern.rs:67-100,226-385`; `packages/tapscript/src/live_pattern.rs:2076-2379,2434-2510`; `packages/tapscript/src/state_constructor.rs:248-333`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:1560-1600`).

37. Wave 5 emits exact pre-link programs whose link-time values are typed consumer requirements, never deployment literals and never a resolution: the opening record maps every consumer to one of the constructor's seven reference declarations or to a Wave-6 reference type that Wave 6 defines, unused symbols are prohibited, and Wave-5 tests may bind explicit checked fixtures but report unresolved consumers rather than claim link closure; the sighash profile is the tree's `OperatorSighashProfile`, and the guide's candidate name for it is recorded as superseded; because Wave 6 owns definitions, binding, relocation, cuts, the residual DAG, ordering and integration, a hard-coded literal would defeat the reference graph, and the identity the code carries is the name (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:1739-1848,3014-3038`; `packages/tapscript/src/state_constructor.rs:608-713,992-1010`; `packages/linker/src/state_constructor_graph.rs:1-18`; `packages/tapscript/src/operator_authorization.rs:318-452`).

38. Guide 14 stays archived verbatim: each disagreement between its Wave-5 text and the tree is recorded as a numbered finding on the card with the tree's name or shape authoritative, and the tree is extended only where a completed Wave-5 consumer proves a missing typed reference or record field; the vectors source comment that says realization exposes no observed-operator-membership decision is corrected to name the seam; the frozen graph's seven references with empty dependency sets are the Wave-4 artifact that Wave 6 extends by consumer census; and the maturity assessment's result that no approved maturity pattern exists stands until the assessment bite replaces it group by group; because a silently selected side makes the coverage and carrier exits uncheckable (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_operator.rs:94-101`; `packages/tapscript/src/maturity_assessment.rs:70-114,285-311`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:1560-1578,1739-1763`).

39. Ruling 24's first-bite rule binds the bite that first yields a complete announcement leaf, not the first Wave-5 code bite: the component fragments land in gate-able bites before the composed program exists, no production static subtree exists until the composed program does, and the bite that composes the program also builds the production subtree, re-runs the constructor derivation and the golden tests over the real leaf and recomputes every golden constant; because a placeholder leaf inside production input is what the rule forbids, a fragment is not a leaf, and a single bite of three thousand lines has no gate-able shape (`plans/phases/06-state-and-maturity.md:184-190`; `packages/tapscript/src/tests/state_constructor_tests.rs:137-172`; `packages/tapscript/src/live_pattern.rs:2246-2510`).

### Wave-6 rulings · `rule:phase6:wave6-rulings`

The Wave-6 opening study's eleven questions and the blocker attached to its third are ruled here on the merits, numbered 40 through 52 and open to revision at the candidate audit like every ruling on this card; the sponsor-shape question is dissolved by ruling 40 rather than answered.

40. An emitted and linked program carries exactly the checks the contract's closure requires and nothing more, so a check beyond closure is a defect of the same kind as a missing one: the covenant's accepted set must equal the contract's permitted set, and a surplus check narrows it, refusing transactions the contract permits while charging symbols to resolve, push sites to bind, leaf bytes and resource weight, and turning an encoding choice into a deployment constraint (`[ADR024-dec:closure:exact-linking]`). The singleton is protected without inspecting any position after zero, because three facts already in the tree carry it: `StateCoordinatorRoleV1` pins the executing input to index zero, so a second STATE object cannot be spent anywhere in the same transaction (`packages/tapscript/src/state_pattern.rs:435-443`); the PID asset is declared with a fixed amount of one and `reissuable: false`, so no input of any transaction can mint it (`packages/architecture/src/spec.rs:489-498`); and target consensus conserves each asset per transaction, so with one PID input and output zero receiving its exact amount every other output carries zero PID, which `StateExternalEvidenceRole::SubstrateConservation` already names rather than re-derives (`packages/tapscript/src/state_pattern.rs:98-117`). The exact input and output counts, the sponsor-suffix asset sweep, the committed change program and version, the fee-program digest, the shape-literal comparison against the sponsor maximum and the per-input issuance sweep are therefore overspecification for closure of the announcement and are removed, and with them the sponsor shape stops being a linked quantity at all, so the study's eleventh question — exact shape against runtime dispatch — is dissolved rather than answered, because it has no subject left. What stays is the self-position pin, input zero's asset, explicit amount and script version, and output zero's asset, explicit amount and authenticated successor program, that last established by the `SuccessorReconstruction` component rather than by the partition (`packages/tapscript/src/state_announcement.rs:519-539`), together with the semantic components and operator authorization; no count bound replaces the removed sweep, because a bound over the whole transaction's inputs is itself a surplus check once composition is permitted, which ruling 52 states in full (`[ADR024-dec:closure:exact-linking]`). Two deployment facts the argument rests on become named external evidence roles beside substrate conservation — the singleton's non-reissuable declaration, and that its issuance placed the whole amount under the constructor, which is the induction base for output closure — because each is a fact about a deployment rather than a fact the leaf checks, and naming them is what keeps the reduction honest. The doctrinal consequence is that the exact-count, sponsor-suffix, change-role, fee-role and no-unclassified-position clauses at (`rule:guide14-exec:coordinator`) and the three census entries `StateCardinalityV1`, `StateSponsorIsolationV1` and `StateIssuanceAbsenceV1` at (`tab:guide14-exec:pattern-census`) are superseded for the STATE generation only, the guide staying archived verbatim under ruling 38 with the supersession recorded here and in the conceptual register; because what those clauses still bought was the exclusion of other operations from the same transaction, and closure is compositional — every family's leaf is locally sound, constraining its own input, its own successor and its own asset, while consensus conserves assets per transaction, so any transaction satisfying every involved leaf satisfies every involved family's invariant, composition in one transaction is permitted rather than excluded, and local soundness becomes the stated obligation of every later family's leaf through Phases 7 to 12. The derivation is published as a discharge table, one row per realization relation of the announcement naming exactly what discharges it, which ruling 48's carrier closure formalizes; the census falls from the eleven symbols ruling 41 leaves to six — structural `StateAsset` and `StateAmount`, semantic `InternalKey`, `MaturityLeadMin` and `MaturityLeadMax`, and the operator's `CommittedOperatorKey` — over a site count the reduction bite recounts and publishes.
41. The structural `StateInputRecognitionV1` fragment stops pushing a literal predecessor program for equality against input zero, keeping its asset, amount and script-version recognition, because that literal cannot be linked: resolved to this constructor's own output program it makes the program depend on its own hash, a cycle with no authenticated cut, and iterating to a fixed point is prohibited at (`rule:guide14-exec:constructor-graph`), while resolving it to an earlier generation contradicts the no-migration continuity rule at (`rule:guide14-exec:bundle-continuity`). Predecessor-program identity is discharged instead by the semantic `MetadataAuthentication` component, which reads the consumed program by introspecting input zero's own witness program and proves it equal to the internal key tweaked over the authenticated metadata and the witnessed root — a strictly stronger check than literal equality, because it binds the program to what commits to it where an equality against a literal compares bytes and nothing else (`packages/tapscript/src/state_announcement.rs:405-431,555-556`). The consequences are stated here rather than discovered later: `StatePatternSymbol` loses `PredecessorProgram` and falls from eight variants to seven (`packages/tapscript/src/state_pattern.rs:74-92`), the composed census falls from twelve symbols over 46 push sites to eleven over 45, the composition prerequisites and census in `packages/tapscript/src/state_program.rs` and their tests change, the golden fixture re-runs over the reduced leaf, finding 39's figures become the before-baseline the recount is read against, and the reopening of a landed Wave-5 consumer is named rather than absorbed. The literal is surplus before it is unlinkable, so this ruling and ruling 40 remove it for the same reason (`[ADR024-rem:closure:consequences]`). The bite carries two of the four extension-point notes of `T11-062`: that `StateConstructorGeneration` is the generation census and a second generation is a distinct recipe identity rather than a metadata change, and that, recognition naming no predecessor under this ruling, generation support would enter through `MetadataAuthentication`'s authentication against the prior generation's recipe — each note stating the current shape's reason and citing the guide's outstanding migration status rather than promising a generations design.
42. The maintained-plans soft target exists to bound reading weight, so the 103 closed Phase-6 rows of the live backlog — 141,507 bytes of section 11 — move verbatim, identifiers retained, into the existing `plans/history/backlog-history.md` under a pointer paragraph, because the relative-link fallback in `packages/labels/src/plans.rs:557-561` already resolves the links the moved rows carry, where a new archive filename would need separate link and census work, and the move carries an explicit preservation audit, because the task-status and duplicate-row checks scan only the live backlog (`packages/labels/src/plans.rs:907-972`) and nothing else verifies the moved rows. Independently the soft target rises from 1,062,912 to 1,228,800 bytes — 1,200 KiB, a whole number of KiB like every constant in this budget — while the hard cap stays 1,572,864 bytes and the ADR and archive caps are unchanged, because seven waves remain in this phase and the Wave-5 opening record alone added 24,139 maintained bytes, so a target that the archived tree re-approaches inside one phase turns the checker into a recurring interruption rather than a signal; the archived tree measures 883,525 bytes, leaving 345,275 bytes of advisory room, and the hard cap still catches runaway growth. Both measures land before this opening record so that the wave is measured against the new figures, ADR-014's hand-managed census is untouched because no file is added or removed, and the landing is `0.6.187-dev` under `T11-049`.
43. STATE gets its own closed linker symbol, value and origin types in a new `state_symbol.rs`, following the live generation's separate symbol family rather than extending compact or live, because extending either would add STATE cases to unrelated bundle and owner roles, while a separate generation derives program identities from the family enums, canonicalizes the shared asset and amount, and keeps stable constructor-reference kinds with values stored apart (`packages/linker/src/live_symbol.rs:122-190`; `packages/tapscript/src/state_constructor.rs:617-641`). The no-unused-symbol check compares definition keys against actual record sites and constructor-field consumers before normalization, with no reserved owner-sighash entry, and a constructor-policy consumer counts even where it produces no script push; under ruling 40 the census this closes over is the reduced one of six symbols, recounted by the reduction bite rather than assumed here, which is what ruling 37 at (`rule:phase6:wave5-rulings`) requires of a pre-link census (`packages/tapscript/src/state_program.rs:63-81`).
44. Definitions are collected from typed, validated sources — the deployment binding, the constructor, the reviewed target and the architecture bounds — in two passes, origin, width, domain and duplicates first and expected types second, never from an untyped value map, because a map loses origin evidence and lets fixture bytes become authority. The existing operator-deployment and lead-bound validation is preserved rather than re-implemented (`packages/linker/src/operator_deployment.rs:124-151`; `packages/realization/src/state.rs:127-139`), and narrow maturity re-exports through tapscript keep the package direction the linker's dependency note fixes (`packages/linker/Cargo.toml:15-40`). The numeric lead bounds 2 and 4 are fixture values rather than calibrated defaults and the definitions say so, because the architecture declares both lead bounds with no default and deployment calibration required (`packages/architecture/src/spec.rs:2029-2038`; `packages/tapscript/src/tests/state_constructor_tests.rs:286`). Under ruling 40 the sponsor-side sources — change program and version, fee digest and the sponsor maximum literal — are not definitions at all, and no sponsor count bound enters the census either, because the leaf carries none.
45. The frozen constructor graph becomes the constructor projection of a complete typed STATE dependency graph: the canonical frozen artifact is retained, its seven reference kinds bind to stable logical keys, the shared 64-reference bound applies to the admitted graph (`packages/linker/src/state_constructor_graph.rs:18-92,136-228`), the witnessed-root equation and its consumer and the actual predecessor tweak authentication are validated as cuts, every validated cut is removed, and the residual graph is proved acyclic with a canonical dependency order — not a cut flag and not one cut per component, either of which leaves other cycles possible (`packages/linker/src/graph.rs:493-537`). Deployment and bound definitions are sources and constructor policy-to-program projections are edges, because the constructor's seven declarations carry empty dependency lists and therefore enumerate nothing on their own (`packages/tapscript/src/state_constructor.rs:994-1010`). Compact's externally outstanding continuity class is not reused as an authenticated STATE proof, because STATE's `MetadataAuthentication` actually authenticates the root and the stronger class is named as such (`packages/linker/src/graph.rs:95-100`); under ruling 41 no self-commitment node exists in this graph, and the extension-point note that a reference to a previously linked bundle is a backward, acyclic edge rather than a cut lives on this graph's class enum. The register rows `G14C-08` and `G14C-09` close on ruling 50's criteria and on nothing weaker.
46. Relocations are discovered from the exact site census cross-checked by one-symbol rebuilding, in a new `state_relocate.rs`: each mandatory instruction or field occurrence has exactly one record carrying logical symbol, expected type, component and leaf role, site or field identity, pre-value, linked value and checked resource delta; each symbol has one definition and each legitimate repeated push its own relocation; input sequences keep duplicates until rejected; shared component aliases cannot double-count a physical site, which the composed program's shared partition range makes a live hazard rather than a hypothetical one (`packages/tapscript/src/state_program.rs:76-81,192-280`); and the final program is rebuilt once from pristine records with every value together, then reparsed and abstractly revalidated (`packages/linker/src/live_relocate.rs:112-165,333-352`). Witnessed roots carry typed runtime-reference evidence and never a literal relocation, because byte search alone finds coincidences and rebuilding alone proves nothing about the census (`rule:guide14-exec:relocations`); the row `G14C-10` takes its contribution from here.
47. STATE admits exactly the ruled singleton static tree and the fixed metadata and static outer pair, with explicit cost evidence, through a new `state_taptree.rs` carrying declaration sequences, exact identity, content, role and version checks, unit weights, the shared checked-cost engine and `SubsetOracleOnly` — the live generation's adapter organization with the compact generation's strict oracle policy (`packages/linker/src/taptree.rs:182-237,303-350,527-592`). One static leaf has no topology choice, so static cost is zero and the unit-weight complete tree costs two, the single leaf being what ruling 34 fixed (`packages/tapscript/src/state_program.rs:522-547`); what is decided here is policy scope, duplicate semantics, depth enforcement and the binding of the evidence to the exact linked leaf bytes, because the constructor recipes already supply paths but neither the oracle comparison nor a deployment depth cap (`packages/tapscript/src/state_constructor.rs:717-737,1031-1040`).
48. Wave 6 publishes checked three-sided carrier closure — compiler, emitted and linked — plus an exact outstanding ABI-role contract, rather than a four-sided claim over placeholder roles: representation, execution case, relation, discharge boundary and selected alternative are compared, vacuity and external requirements are preserved, actual component ranges are verified inside the committed leaf, and duplicate, missing, extra and unreachable assignments are rejected in both directions, with Wave 7 supplying the fourth side against the same keys as its own task at (`task:guide14-exec:wave7`) requires (`packages/compiler/src/operation_plan.rs:230-300`; `packages/tapscript/src/maturity_assessment.rs:764-790,830-944`). Under ruling 40 the closure's row set is the discharge table: one row per realization relation of the announcement, 26 in all, each naming its discharge as a positional check, self-position, consensus conservation, a named external evidence role or a semantic component, so the sponsor relations close by substrate and self-position rather than by positional carriers; the guide's four-sided wording at (`rule:guide14-exec:carrier-closure`) therefore receives its staged interpretation on this card rather than in prose elsewhere, and the 26 relations with their 24 emitted carriers and two external ones are the census that reading is checked against, as recorded at (`sec:phase6:wave5-findings`) and asserted in the projection's own test (`packages/tapscript/src/tests/maturity_assessment_tests.rs:1058-1066`).
49. The linked result is a new `CandidateLinkedMaturityBundle` in `state_bundle.rs`, following the live generation's candidate organization: exact plan and reviewed target, validated deployment and operator binding, constructor policy and exact static subtree, linked record and programs, placements and control recipes, definitions, graph and cut proof, relocations, checked resources, carrier closure, ABI obligations, evidence and lifecycle obligations, and candidate status, with no content digest, against the definition at (`def:guide14-exec:linked-bundle`) and the live generation's own aggregate (`packages/linker/src/live_bundle.rs:443-465`). A concrete constructor is metadata- and nonce-specific, so the aggregate exposes a fallible constructor application over the fixed linked subtree and retains supplied concrete instances only with their exact metadata (`packages/tapscript/src/state_constructor.rs:780-889`), while Wave 7 owns current-state validation and nonce search at (`rule:guide14-exec:successor-search`) and finalization and witness population at (`rule:guide14-exec:finalization`). The continuity equality of §17.3 — predecessor static subtree equals successor static subtree — is one named check on this bundle, because it is the single assertion a migration relation would replace and scattered equalities would leave nothing for a later generation to point at.
50. The conceptual rows close only on their explicit adversarial criteria: `G14C-08` on a residual-DAG proof together with a component carrying two edge-disjoint cycles where one authenticated cut is insufficient and the linker must refuse; `G14C-09` on closed binding-time forms and the refusal of a literal static root beneath itself, with exact witnessed-root authentication; and `G14C-10` on stable definition, edge, component, relocation and equal-cost-tree identities plus simultaneous pristine rebuilding and reparsing under permutations. The graph, relocation and bundle tests carry these cases and the register closes each row on that gate evidence rather than on broad success tests, because the current cycle policy accepts a component as soon as one classified edge resolves a cycle (`packages/linker/src/graph.rs:503-509`) and the constructor's declarations carry empty dependency lists (`packages/tapscript/src/state_constructor.rs:994-1010`), so a success-only suite would leave each row's central failure mode untested.
51. Linked resource and capability evidence is recomputed as checked linked totals beside the existing abstract schedule and final-stack walk, with exact tables for the one linked program, and explicit absent native observations, in a new `state_resource.rs`; it does not inherit the record's three-dimensional saturating diagnostic projection, which cannot establish resource or capability closure (`packages/tapscript/src/stack.rs:1666-1697`), and the prerequisite adapter's missing hash, domain and resource treatment stays visible rather than being quietly covered (`packages/tapscript/src/pattern.rs:1220-1247`). The live generation's exact measurement and refit discipline is followed (`packages/linker/src/live_resource.rs:43-78,182-233`) without importing receipt axes or promoting an incomplete generic sighash capability, and the pieces still missing are those already recorded at (`sec:phase6:wave5-findings`). Under ruling 40 there is no admitted shape set to index those tables by, because the shape is no longer a linked quantity.
52. The realization evaluates five of the announcement's relations over the whole observed transaction rather than over the positions the operation claims: `AllowedObjectFamilies { side, allowed }` accepts only when every observed object on that side belongs to an allowed family, `SponsorIsolation` reads the observation's flows entire, `CanonicalDeltaPolicy` compares the transaction's canonical partition, `OpenFlowPolicy` quantifies over every open flow the transaction carries, and `SponsorEnvelopeMultiplicity` counts the transaction's sponsor flows (`packages/realization/src/evaluate.rs:414-493`; `packages/realization/src/relation.rs:56-95`). Under ruling 40 the covenant accepts a transaction that also carries another family's operation under that family's own leaf, so a model that keeps those five relations as written refuses what the covenant accepts — the accepted set exceeds the permitted set, which the closure-exact principle forbids in that direction exactly as it forbids the other. An operation's transaction-side relations therefore become statements about the region the operation claims, with region membership part of the observation and a transaction the union of its operations' regions; the five relations are re-scoped by that refit and `SponsorEnvelopeMultiplicity` is retired or re-scoped with them, since it is resource policy rather than semantics, and meanwhile the leaf carries no count bound for it, because a transaction-wide bound is itself a surplus check under composition. Adoption is staged, because the leaf reduction is safe without the refit while the refit is not needed for safety: the reduction bite lands first, since every check it removes is one the singleton's safety argument does not need and the model meanwhile continues to describe single-operation transactions; the discharge table classes those five relations MODEL-SCOPE — evaluated by the realization over the whole observed transaction, enforced by no leaf, retired by the refit — so the gap is named on every published closure rather than hidden; and the region-scoping refit is its own bite under `T11-064`, Wave-6 scope if a Wave-6 link depends on a closed carrier for those relations and otherwise the first bite of Phase 7's composition work, because the first composed operation is what exercises it. Until that refit lands a composed transaction is accepted on-chain and outside the model, and the closure record says so; the ADR's header and decision already record the changed scope of the five relations and the staged refit (`[ADR024-dec:closure:exact-linking]`).

### Wave-7 rulings · `rule:phase6:wave7-rulings`

The Wave-7 opening study's nine design questions are ruled here on the merits, numbered 53 through 61 and open to revision at the candidate audit like every ruling on this card; they are taken over that study rather than over the wave's task text alone at (`task:guide14-exec:wave7`), because the guide names a request type the tree does not carry and an index width the tree outgrew, and because the wave's first exit turns on a fact about the deployments in the tree rather than on code still to be written.

53. The target's default relay policy admits no tapscript stack item wider than eighty bytes, while the announcement's `PredecessorMetadata` witness role declares the eighty-six the metadata codec fixes, so an announcement spend carrying real metadata is not relayable under default policy and is judged only at the block layer (`packages/vectors/tests/maturity_closure.rs:653`; `packages/realization/src/state_codec.rs:25`; `packages/tapscript/src/tests/state_program_tests.rs:177-206`). The STATE ABI publishes that as a typed reviewed-form verdict — relay admission refused for the announcement form, direct submission to a producer named as the route, consensus admission unchanged — rather than as a comment, because eighty-six exceeds eighty unconditionally and no fee, package or version choice lifts a stack-item width limit. The compact generation's precedent therefore does not transfer: selecting the topology-restricted transaction version for the sponsorless form turns a recorded relay condition into a constructible path exactly because that form's reviewed relay verdict is admitted-under-condition and one of its two conditions is a topology-restricted package, which is a policy choice over a form the policy still admits, where a width limit leaves no condition to select (`packages/target-elements/src/transaction_form.rs:223-257,304-323`; `packages/transaction/src/abi.rs:188-216,764-767`). The eighty-byte figure moves out of the vectors test into the reviewed target's own vocabulary beside its relay conditions with its provenance named, so that a policy fact the ABI publishes has a first-party definition rather than a test constant as its only source. Splitting the item into relay-admissible pieces the leaf reassembles is a named candidate for whichever wave next opens the leaf and not this wave's: it would move the witness schedule, its widths and order, the authentication's slicing, the leaf's bytes, the golden constants, the resource totals, the kept checks and the obligation contract — most of what Wave 6 closed.
54. Exit 1 needs a signature that actually verifies, which (`rule:guide14-exec:operator-authorization`) requires and which `authorize_operator` enforces by refusing through an independent verifier rather than by inspecting the key it was handed (`packages/transaction/src/operator_signing.rs:566-592`); both STATE deployments in the tree commit an operator key of public meaningless material — a thirty-two-byte fill beside a singleton fill of the same kind and a fixture lead window — so a submit-ready candidate over either is unreachable by construction, nobody holding either scalar (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_closure.rs:291-335`). Parameterizing the vectors' maturity sources by singleton asset, committed operator key and lead window as supplied values is therefore the wave's first code bite, those three being per-deployment constants of a two-member census today: a third deployment commits the x-only key of the third published test signer handle, because ruling 17 at (`rule:phase6:wave3-rulings`) reserves the first and third published scalars for the positive and the wrong-key controls and the operator carrier already binds the third, which leaves the first as the wrong-key control (`packages/target-elements-conformance/src/test_material.rs:124-129`; `packages/vectors/src/maturity_operator.rs:679`); the two existing deployments and the six golden roots stay bit-identical, so the parameterization is proved by what it does not move (`packages/vectors/tests/maturity_closure.rs:70`). The bite belongs in vectors and not in transaction: (`rule:guide14-exec:signer-ownership`) puts the statement that a public signer is the authorized maturity operator in the vectors layer, ruling 17 confines the material to the conformance layer, and the transaction crate records the conformance package's absence as load-bearing — that package is the independent host oracle this crate's output is compared against — while `vectors` is forbidden to it outright (`packages/transaction/Cargo.toml:27-32`; `plans/packages/transaction.md:34-51`).
55. The public current-STATE view of (`def:guide14-exec:state-view`) is caller-supplied public facts, and the ABI validates internal consistency exactly: the supplied predecessor metadata and representation nonce, encoded canonically under (`rule:guide14-exec:canonical-metadata`) and committed under the bundle's constructor policy over the linked static subtree, must reproduce the supplied predecessor program, which is the leaf's own authentication equation computed first-party rather than taken on the caller's word (`packages/tapscript/src/state_announcement.rs:396-448`). Freshness — that the outpoint is the current root — is established by no layer here: it is the external evidence role the structural record already declares and the bridge deliberately does not record, and requiring a chain observation would import the branch machinery the register assigns to Wave 10 under `G14C-11` at (`task:guide14-exec:wave10`) (`packages/tapscript/src/state_pattern.rs:98-117`). The link obligation `CurrentStateValidationUndischarged` is therefore split at the ABI rather than met by prose or carried whole: the reconstruction half is discharged by computation and the freshness half is carried as a narrower named residual in the ABI's own outstanding set, while the linker's obligation enum is unchanged, because a linked bundle's obligations state what that link owes and not what a later layer discharged (`packages/linker/src/state_bundle.rs:143-152`).
56. The successor search runs through `CandidateLinkedMaturityBundle::apply_constructor` and never as a second scan in the transaction layer: one owner for the search and its budget — the reviewed bounded scan from zero — with leastness evidence returned inside the constructor application and the continuity equality of (`rule:guide14-exec:bundle-continuity`) held by `retain`, because a second scan gives the leastness policy and its measured budget a second place to drift (`packages/linker/src/state_bundle.rs:706,736`; `packages/tapscript/src/state_constructor.rs:523-540`). A failed search reaches the ABI as the linker's own refusal wrapped in one transaction refusal variant rather than flattened into a generic construction failure, so exhaustion, a wrong nonce and a later-admissible nonce stay distinguishable, the last two by name as (`tab:guide14-exec:successor-faults`) requires; and the Wave-7 clause of `G14C-03` — host-policy refusal without a claim of target invalidity — is discharged by exactly that exhaustion refusal, a budget the host set being the host's policy and no statement about what the target would accept.
57. Finalization and signing get a STATE-specific chain shaped on the live generation's rather than an extension of the live types, whose owner plurality and confidential-proof operations have no STATE subject: five typed states — the finalized transfer, the proof-finalized candidate, signing started, the fully authorized candidate and the submit-ready candidate — whose arrows are type transitions and not flags, so a candidate cannot be in two states and no field can disagree with one (`packages/transaction/src/live_finalize.rs:55-97`; `packages/transaction/src/live_materialize.rs:1813`; `packages/transaction/src/live_handoff.rs:50-59,201,468,545`). The protected-region census is twelve members in the order (`rule:guide14-exec:finalization`) states them: inputs, the spent-output census, outputs, the successor metadata, the successor representation nonce, the successor program, output witnesses, version, lock time, the sponsor region, the fee region and the executing leaf data — the live census's eight regions less its confidential nonce fields, which have no STATE subject, plus the five this operation owns (`packages/transaction/src/live_materialize.rs:1437-1468`; `packages/transaction/src/live_census.rs:381-391`). Every protected operation from signing-started onward refuses by naming its region, so the census can be walked by a test rather than believed, and the four post-signing mutations of (`rule:guide14-exec:signing-faults`) — metadata, successor program, sponsor input and fee role — are named explicitly, because a refusal that the exact echo decides only as an undifferentiated wrong candidate is evidence that something moved rather than that a named mutation rejects; the operator module's statement that those four need no separate mutation variants stands for its own synthetic scope, where the echo is the whole question (`packages/transaction/src/operator_signing.rs:425-429`).
58. Wave 7 builds the sponsorless candidate, and the sponsored arm stays representable in the typed request while construction refuses it by a named typed refusal citing the absent carrier: under ruling 40 the reduced leaf carries no sponsor check at all, and under ruling 52 the realization's sponsor relations are model-scope until the region-scoping refit of `T11-064` gives them a region, so a builder emitting sponsored bytes this wave would emit a form that no leaf and no model constrains (`def:guide14-exec:typed-request`). The wave's second exit is therefore met conditionally, with the condition stated in the closure record rather than by enforcing the compact or live sponsor checks as though they were this generation's, because that exit asks for the sponsored form only where inherited policy claims it (`task:guide14-exec:wave7`).
59. The ABI lives in `packages/transaction` and admits no new dependency: the crate already names linker, realization, tapscript and target-elements, which is exactly the package card's allowed list; the direction realization → tapscript → linker → transaction is the card's own; the ABI evaluates no formula, so the clause admitting realization only where linked construction recipes cannot supply formula evaluation is untouched; and the conformance package stays absent by design, its absence load-bearing because that package is the independent host oracle this crate's output is compared against, so a builder computing its expectation by calling it would produce one opinion and count it twice (`plans/packages/transaction.md:34-58`; `packages/transaction/Cargo.toml:15-37`). The signer-held-key positive of ruling 54 lives in `packages/vectors` for the same reason read from the other side: `vectors` is forbidden to this crate outright.
60. Submit-ready is a state of a candidate and never a promotion, the ABI result being the candidate form that (`rule:guide14-exec:abi-status`) names and carrying no digest with it: a single-variant status enum read and never written, no digest field and no place for one, a derive that refuses a bundle claiming more than the prototype artifact status, a structurally non-empty outstanding set whose least obligation is a field of its own, and a submit-ready type holding the candidate bytes and the accepted authorization as the live generation's does — the two landed generations' settlement copied exactly rather than re-argued (`packages/linker/src/bundle.rs:65-72`; `packages/linker/src/state_bundle.rs:198-201,684-690`; `packages/transaction/src/live_handoff.rs:545`). No final type appears anywhere, because (`rule:guide14-exec:candidate-only`) lists both what this guide may produce and what it must not, and the candidate stays lifecycle-incomplete while later STATE operations are absent.
61. The ABI's native exercise extends the four-shape observation carrier that landed with `0.6.198-dev` rather than replacing it: a fifth case built over a submit-ready candidate is read beside the four the target refuses at the committed operator key, and that neighbourhood is what makes passing the fifth meaningful, because the four establish that the chain from linked bytes through static subtree, output key and control block is the one the target actually runs. It needs three things this wave supplies or fixes: a node-issued singleton, so the parameterized sources of ruling 54 accept a runtime asset; the signer of ruling 54, whose scalar a handle holds; and the relay verdict of ruling 53, because an announcement spend will report as a consensus refusal carrying the policy reason where the adapter decides the observed layer from the mempool reason alone, which finding 64 at (`sec:phase6:wave6-findings`) records (`packages/vectors/tests/maturity_closure.rs`). The native run is Wave 8's evidence at (`task:guide14-exec:wave8`); Wave 7's own exit is the node-free positive.

### Wave-8 rulings · `rule:phase6:wave8-rulings`

The Wave-8 opening study's eight design questions are ruled here on the merits, numbered 62 through 69 and open to revision at the candidate audit like every ruling on this card; they are taken over that study rather than over the wave's task text alone at (`task:guide14-exec:wave8`), because three of the eight inputs the evidence plan is built from do not exist in the tree and two of those index material later waves produce, because the fifth native case cannot be observed as an acceptance under the executor as it stands, and because the guide assigns deliverables to waves and never rows, so every row-to-wave mapping the wave acts on is an inference a record has to make rather than a reading it can take.

62. `MaturityAnnouncementEvidencePlan` is derived from all eight inputs of (`def:guide14-exec:evidence-plan`) and from nothing else, with private fields and no public constructor accepting a caller-authored verdict, which is that definition's own closing condition. Five inputs exist in the tree — the validated compiler operation plan, the exact linked bundle, the exact candidate ABI, the exact target and deployment binding, and the expected executor provenance — and three do not: the canonical semantic fixture registry this wave builds under ruling 69, and the canonical constructor and root-history mutation registries, whose material belongs to the constructor projections of (`task:guide14-exec:wave9`) and the root-history report of (`task:guide14-exec:wave10`). The two later-wave registries are therefore built now as structurally non-empty registries that are explicitly outstanding — each carrying a typed reason for its emptiness and a census counting zero mutations under a named standing — rather than left out of the derivation. The precedent is the tree's own, twice. The candidate ABI carries `CurrentStateRootFreshnessUnestablished` rather than dropping it, because an obligation carried whole would understate what was computed and one dropped would overstate it (`packages/transaction/src/state_abi.rs:513-529`). The live evidence keeps `carried_residuals` as a typed function returning an empty set, named as a set rather than as prose so that a residual entering or leaving is an edit to the one place a reader can find, and so that an empty set states a discharge rather than a smaller obligation (`packages/vectors/src/live_evidence.rs:2935-2982`). A plan built from five inputs would not be the plan §14.1 defines, and a plan deferred until its last input exists would leave the wave's first exit with no derivation behind it.

63. §16's thirteen tables at (`sec:guide14-exec:vectors`) are transcribed once, into one STATE safety matrix with one census and one denominator, and the denominator is 206 rows at this tip: 14, 11, 16, 18, 15, 15, 14, 16, 15, 14, 23, 22 and 13.
