The 16.3 figure is the eleven faults the table lists plus one row for each semantic field the announcement leaves unaffected, because `StateMetadata` carries six fields and the transition moves maturity alone, copying the other five through (`packages/realization/src/state.rs:53-71,302-315`; `packages/model/src/tests/state_metadata_conformance_tests.rs:170`). Every row carries all nine facts of §16's preamble — polarity, mutation layer, declared evidence boundary, intended relation, intended carrier, collateral closure, canonical control, mutation locator and expected projection — which is wider than the live row, whose seven fields are a section and a name plus five of the nine, leaving the intended carrier, the canonical control, the mutation locator and the expected projection with nowhere to sit (`packages/vectors/src/live_safety.rs:363-371`). A row another wave answers does not stand outstanding: it carries a typed non-answer member naming the reason, on the pattern of the live row boundary, whose one layer member sits beside four typed closures (`packages/vectors/src/live_safety.rs:329-341`). Four such reasons are already determined. The sponsor rows of 16.10 stand where ruling 58 left the sponsored arm, refused by name under rulings 40 and 52 until the region-scoping refit of `T11-064` gives the sponsor relations a region. The relay-refused announcement rows stand under ruling 64's standing. The root-history rows of 16.8 are answered by the report Wave 10 owns at (`task:guide14-exec:wave10`). The nonce and tweak totality rows of 16.7 are answered by Wave 4's landed evidence under `R-1 NONCE` in `plans/guides/guide_fourteen_conceptual_register.md`. Two waves transcribing one guide section into two tables would reproduce, at the specification level, the defect the boundary rule's own record names: two consumers of one semantic rule, one of which had no access to it, is how seven wrong-boundary rows came to stand as answered (`packages/vectors/src/observed_boundary.rs:10-19`).

64. The native run is Wave 8's evidence and not its exit, as ruling 61 states, and §23's seventh conjunct — one sponsorless positive control accepted by a real target — is the Phase-6 gate's at (`gate:guide14-exec:exit`), recorded by this wave as not met. The executor as it stands asks the mempool first, returns a script verdict where the refusal names one, and otherwise offers the transaction to `generateblock`: when that block accepts, the observation is `relay_policy_rejection` carrying the mempool's reason, and when the block also refuses, the mempool's reason decides and the observation is a consensus refusal before script (`scripts/elements-native-executor.py:4584-4664,4666-4722`). The width the announcement meets is standardness measured before execution, not consensus, so the block accepts and the spend is mined (`packages/target-elements/src/transaction_form.rs:253-283`). The submission subject declares no layer, no expected identity and no class, and schema 8 forbids an accepted identity or a mined readback on any observation but `accepted` (`packages/target-elements-conformance/src/protocol.rs:229,2160-2172,3215-3226`). The fifth case therefore runs this wave with its positive row declaring `RelayPolicyRejection` as its boundary, which establishes that the whole chain from linked bytes through static subtree, output key, control block and the committed key's signature reaches the relay boundary and is stopped there by a width and nothing else; and the accepted row stands explicitly outstanding with its two routes named — the relay restructure the ABI already carries as `RelayAdmissibleWitnessSplitUnopened` (`packages/transaction/src/state_abi.rs:530-541`), and a protocol revision adding a block-layer submission subject, which ruling 19 shows the shape of: both sides move together, the schema bumps, and the historical contract stays parsed. A relaxed node policy is not a third route. The node's argument list carries no standardness relaxation today (`scripts/elements-native-executor.py:2182-2204`), an acceptance under a policy nobody deploys is not the acceptance §23 asks for, and every artifact carrying it would have to carry the caveat with it.

65. Four generation-neutral types are reused rather than copied: `EvidenceBoundary` and `MutationLayer` (`packages/vectors/src/matrix.rs:150-161,222-247`), `observed_boundary` with `matches_boundary` (`packages/vectors/src/observed_boundary.rs:59-99`), and the canonical and experimental subjects (`packages/vectors/src/subject.rs:60,107`). None carries a live subject — two are a specification transcribed into types, one is a total mapping over an observation vocabulary, and the last is a pair of generic wrappers — and `observed_boundary` was extracted precisely so that one rule has one implementation, which is the reason a second consumer must read it rather than restate it. The rows, standings, plan and report are STATE-specific, on ruling 57's argument read precisely: the live types carry owner plurality and confidential-proof operations that have no STATE subject. The live types are not generified for a second consumer, because the six modules that carry the live rows, standings, discharge, register and report run to 16,990 lines of landed and gated evidence at this tip, and a type parameter threaded through them would put every one of those lines back through review for the benefit of a generation that can state its own rows.

66. Wave 8 implements the closed three-class observation vocabulary and the class-3 poison marker, and places the marker beside the STATE standings so that no standing member can accept it — a compile-time guarantee where the same statement in a report is a runtime check — states every Wave-8 guarantee with the class-1 and class-2 and no-class-3 quantifier `G14C-12` requires, and carries one class-3 test: a model-falsifying byte observation with no abstraction preimage. The typed class-2 responses and the three environment tests — competing spend, same-key instance, reorganization-surfaced realization — are Wave 10's under `R-2 CANONICALITY` and `G14C-11`, because their subject is a branch context whose typed projection, rewind and reprojection Wave 10 delivers at (`task:guide14-exec:wave10`) and this wave has no way to observe. Leaving the row wholly to Wave 10 is not open to this wave: the register assigns `G14C-12` to Waves 8 and 10 together, and a wave cannot pass its exit with an owned row untouched.

67. The mutation locator and the intended carrier are carried on the row, and the accepted control's identity is carried on the standing. The first two are properties of a row's design and belong in a file that holds no bytes and observes nothing, which is what keeps the specification falsifiable rather than self-confirming (`packages/vectors/src/live_safety.rs:33-38`); the third is a target-computed fact, and a reader who does not trust the crate must be able to check it against the run. The four refusal fields of (`rule:guide14-exec:standing`) that the live standing lacks are added to the STATE standing: the submitted subject identity or exact bytes, the mutation locator or structural separator, the intended carrier, and whether the carrier executed. The live standing carries the other four — declared boundary, observed layer, control identity and refusal detail — and carries them on the observed refusal where they were computed (`packages/vectors/src/live_evidence.rs:687-722`). The division answers §1.15's five conditions where each is decidable: a row's declared boundary, its locator and its intended carrier before anything executes, and the accepted control and the exact submitted bytes only after.

68. The safety report witnesses the soundness clause of `G14C-02` and states it in its own type: every refused concrete step corresponds to no allowed semantic step at its declared boundary. That is the second half of the question (`def:guide14-exec:safety-report`) asks, and it is the half this wave can answer — the first half, that valid announcements preserved the exact semantic operation, wants an accepted step, and the forward clause the register states in full wants one too, together with the predecessor and successor constructor projections Wave 9 delivers at (`task:guide14-exec:wave9`). The forward clause is therefore Wave 9's and the abstraction relation is not published this wave. Witnessing one clause exactly is what the register asks each report to do; naming a clause nobody has written would be weaker than witnessing one, and §1.14 forbids the report from satisfying another class by implication.

69. The canonical semantic fixture registry takes the realization transition now — `announce_maturity`, which is total and copies every unaffected field through (`packages/realization/src/state.rs:302-315`) — and cites the model-conformance tests as the standing evidence that the executable model agrees, where the agreement table walks minimum and maximum lead, both endpoints' overflow, the already-announced and complete refusals and the projected successor (`packages/model/src/tests/state_metadata_conformance_tests.rs:246-461`). The second and third producers of (`rule:guide14-exec:expected-semantics`) are recorded on the registry as explicitly outstanding producers rather than silently absent, because one of them cannot be called at all: the typed projection adapter exists only as private functions of a test module the model crate compiles under `cfg(test)` (`packages/model/src/lib.rs:62-63`; `packages/model/src/tests/state_metadata_conformance_tests.rs:32,58`), and the evidence crate does not depend on the model crate, which its manifest declines deliberately for want of a call (`packages/vectors/Cargo.toml:29-33,115-123`). Whether admitting that crate needs a dependency record is the wave's named residual, handed up with its corrected citation: the package contract at (`pkg:vectors:contract`) and the package-creation rule at (`rule:guide12-exec:vectors-package`) both already name `model` among this package's expected direct dependencies, while the eight conditions of (`rule:backlog:dependency-entry`) are written for third-party dependencies. The backend constructor generates no expected semantics, as §15.3 requires and as the compact registry already practises (`packages/vectors/src/fixture.rs:11-19`).

### Wave-9 rulings · `rule:phase6:wave9-rulings`

The Wave-9 opening study's nine design questions are ruled here on the merits, numbered 70 through 78 and open to revision at the candidate audit like every ruling on this card, with the route's ruling 79 ratified beside them at the route record; they are taken over that study rather than over the wave's task text alone at (`task:guide14-exec:wave9`), because its first exit cannot be met at this tip — the corpus holds no accepted submission with target identity and mined readback, and the executor's block fallback is not the accepted observation schema 8 defines — because the validated continuity report is absent and every other deliverable is partial substrate, and because the guide assigns deliverables to waves and never rows, so the mapping onto independently gated bites is an inference this record must state (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_corpus.rs`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_evidence.rs`, `packages/vectors/src/lib.rs`, `scripts/elements-native-executor.py`, `packages/target-elements-conformance/src/protocol.rs`).

70. Wave 9 opens node-free over the two byte sources already present: the submit-ready candidate of `0.6.210-dev` in `packages/vectors/tests/maturity_abi.rs` and the admitted archived submission of `0.6.227-dev` under `packages/vectors/fixtures/maturity-run-of-record/`, loaded by `packages/vectors/src/maturity_corpus.rs`. Its projections, comparisons and report carry acceptance as a typed unmet condition, so the first exit at (`task:guide14-exec:wave9`) stands NOT MET until accepted readback with target identity exists, just as §23's seventh conjunct remains outstanding at (`gate:guide14-exec:exit`). Neither acceptance route is taken here: the relay-witness split must move the ABI schedule, witness population, authentication slicing, linked leaf commitments, goldens and resource accounting together, as `RelayAdmissibleWitnessSplitUnopened` in `packages/transaction/src/state_abi.rs` records; the block-layer submission subject must change both protocol peers, response validation, native planning and capture admission and produce new run evidence, as the two `MaturityAcceptanceRoute` members in `packages/vectors/src/maturity_native.rs` distinguish. Each route needs its own closed implementation plan and neither is a continuity deliverable. The same exact-byte continuity producer built node-free can consume an accepted readback later, so this work remains useful, whereas blocking the whole wave on a route would deliver none of it. Finding 96's block fallback reports relay refusal with no accepted identity and cannot replace schema 8's accepted submission and mined readback (`scripts/elements-native-executor.py:4650-4664`, `packages/target-elements-conformance/src/protocol.rs:3208-3225`). `T11-098` therefore carries acceptance as a separately ruled dependency, with no route selected and no node run scheduled by this record.

71. The semantic projection's producer is the realization transition, following ruling 69: `announce_maturity` is total and copies the five unaffected fields through `StateMetadata::with_maturity`, so the expected successor is derived from each byte source's actual typed inputs (`packages/realization/src/state.rs:53-90,302-345`). Both the native planner and the node-free candidate use predecessor cycle 5, bounds 4–6 and requested cycle 10; the sponsorless semantic fixture instead requests 9 under bounds 3–5 (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_native.rs:533-638`, `packages/vectors/tests/maturity_abi.rs:87-116`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_fixture.rs:151-155,882-940`). A row label identifies a case, not its expected value: looking up that fixture by the shared sponsorless name would conceal different transition inputs, while `semantic_case` derives the expectation from the supplied predecessor, bounds and request (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_fixture.rs:991-1026`). Model agreement is cited under its actual premises, including sealing, operator authorization and fee/world conditions, which the conformance path exercises rather than erases (`packages/model/src/ops/maturity.rs:31-79`, `packages/model/src/tests/state_metadata_conformance_tests.rs:246-283`). The executable-model and typed-adapter producers remain explicitly outstanding: the adapter is private test code under `cfg(test)`, the vectors manifest has no model dependency, and adding that dependency would decide finding 97's first-party dependency-record question without a continuity deliverable needing it decided (`packages/model/src/lib.rs:62-63`, `packages/model/src/tests/state_metadata_conformance_tests.rs:32-82`, `packages/vectors/Cargo.toml`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_fixture.rs:774-802`). A full abstraction theorem still needs a separately reviewed adapter and environment relation.

72. Continuity has a dedicated validated report in its own module and with its own report role, following the safety report's assemble/validate/render pattern: private assembly fields, a validation witness, rendering from the validated wrapper alone, and the three completeness states and eleven volatile categories reused as types (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_report.rs:44-133,939-959,1241-1255`). The fifteenth conjunct at (`gate:guide14-exec:exit`) keeps safety, continuity, history, recovery and resources separate, and §1.14 at (`rule:guide14-exec:evidence-separation`) forbids one report satisfying another by implication. The two-sided fields and independent equality results at (`def:guide14-exec:continuity-report`) consequently cannot borrow the safety role. An aggregate STATE report would need an independently validated continuity subreport with its own completeness anyway; flattening those results into safety would erase the very distinction the gate checks. A later aggregate container may hold validated reports without merging their evidence.

73. `ConstructorContinuityObserved` is reserved for the accepted-byte observation: a validated comparison over accepted readback bytes with target-computed identity. Validated host comparisons over submit-ready and archived submission bytes are first-party continuity records carrying source class, branch context, row identity and byte identity; they are associated with their exact rows and never enter that bucket, and no target-declared row changes boundary merely because a host comparison exists. The standing is already among the answered members of `MaturityRowStanding`, and `is_answered` counts it (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_evidence.rs:985-1139`). Putting a node-free comparison there would give accepted-sounding weight to evidence with no target identity, the same category error Wave 8's third exit excludes when it keeps infrastructure results from becoming target verdicts at (`sec:phase6:wave8-findings`). The constructor registry's always-outstanding representation becomes a typed material-present/absent input, while the root-history registry remains outstanding because it still lacks branch-indexed projection (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_evidence.rs:358-414,484-520`). Observation class is carried separately from byte-source class under ruling 76; the class-3 poison marker can enter no continuity standing, by type.

74. For `G14C-02`'s forward clause and `G14C-14`, Wave 9 records the concrete projection functions, the codec laws they use and conditional forward-comparison evidence over exact bytes, while both rows remain OPEN (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen_conceptual_register.md`). Those laws include exact round trips of semantic-plus-representation values and canonical byte strings, erasure of the nonce from semantic projection, and the `TapLeaf/elements`, `TapBranch/elements` and `TapTweak/elements` domains under named collision-resistance and second-preimage assumptions (`packages/realization/src/state_codec.rs`, `packages/tapscript/src/state_constructor.rs:167-224`). Exact byte equality, commitment-root equality under those assumptions and hidden-subtree-content equality remain three distinct claims: the linker compares the retained tree because its descriptor identities are not all committed by the root (`packages/linker/src/state_bundle.rs:373-411`). A test over one transition can establish that the actual submitted bytes decode and reconstruct the expected constructors under stated premises; it cannot prove the universal forward clause or infer an unknown hidden subtree's contents from matching roots. Closing either row on local tests would conflate an implemented relation with its accepted-step witness. The shared obligations remain the accepted-step forward witness, typed abstraction relation and supported-step completeness under named representability assumptions for `G14C-02`, and the universal codec, field-layout and commitment laws with their explicit assumptions for `G14C-14`.

75. The equal-metadata/different-static and same-static/wrong-successor negatives are coherent bundle pairs rebuilt from deployment recipes, with independently retained expected trees and separately controlled semantic fields; parser and control failures also receive explicitly EXPERIMENTAL byte mutations. Every mutant records its original matrix boundary and actual carrier, and ruling 63's single matrix at (`sec:guide14-exec:vectors`) acquires no new row (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_safety.rs`). The cases include retained-tree mismatch, same-hash descriptor-identity mismatch where representable, stale constructor, program and nonce substitutions, and wrong control parity and path. A foreign malformed leaf fails too early to isolate continuity, while changing the deployment moves metadata context and linked static content together, as the deployment and relocation tests show; coherent pairs keep those axes independently falsifiable (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_closure.rs:380-395`, `packages/linker/src/tests/state_bundle_tests.rs:390-460`). Equal metadata cannot excuse differing static trees at (`rule:guide14-exec:bundle-continuity`), and a valid control must mask neither static nor semantic disagreement. Branch-stale freshness remains with Wave 10 at (`task:guide14-exec:wave10`), because bundle inequality establishes no branch lifecycle fact.

76. Every continuity guarantee carries ruling 66's two-class quantifier: over class 1 and class 2, conditional on no class-3 observation. Each observation carries typed branch identity and checkpoint as caller-stated context with no freshness claim, as finding 136 records for the admitted run (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_evidence.rs:214-352`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_corpus.rs:1167-1230`). Implemented intended examples are distinguished from still-unobserved modeled environment transitions; a declared quantifier is not evidence that competing spend, same-key instance or reorganization has been exercised. The poison marker is excluded from every continuity standing by type, rather than converted into an ordinary failure. Wave 10 keeps branch lifecycle and the competing-transition producers under `R-2 CANONICALITY` and `G14C-11` in `plans/guides/guide_fourteen_conceptual_register.md`, because a caller's branch operands supply context for a comparison and cannot authenticate the context's freshness.

77. The retained-predecessor coherence risk is answered in the continuity projector this wave: it reconstructs the witnessed predecessor from submitted bytes and the funded spent output and refuses disagreement among witness, spent output and retained context. An adversarial coherent-alternate-instance test also characterizes the transaction view. Its validator reconstructs the offered metadata/nonce/program triple under the bundle but enforces no equality against the first retained metadata tuple, while finalization's executing leaf and signing's predecessor parity consult that first retained constructor (`packages/transaction/src/state_view.rs:430-465`, `packages/transaction/src/state_finalize.rs`, `packages/transaction/src/state_signing.rs:935-1000`). This locates a missing comparison without reproducing a reachable defect. A projector must refuse inconsistent evidence to make its own report true, whereas a semantic restriction on the public view would change transaction API behavior and needs its own ruling over a demonstrated failure. If the characterization reproduces one, it becomes a finding and a transaction-crate row, never a silent fix inside the projector.

78. Wave 9 expands no constructor schema: `CandidateStateConstructor` and `StateStaticLeaf` gain neither a leaf-weight field nor caller-order retention. Normalized branch order remains a tested invariant, finding 122's absent weight term remains an explicitly carried row, and continuity fixtures supply finding 121's missing retained expected subtree, the residual directly needed for the comparison (`packages/tapscript/src/state_constructor.rs:197-213,348-389`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_first_party.rs:3054-3073`). A discharge obtained by inventing a mutation of a nonexistent weight term would answer another question, and retaining caller order solely to reject it would replace normalization with new API behavior. The scripted curve stubs remain in `packages/linker/src/tests/mod.rs` and `packages/linker/src/tests/state_taptree_tests.rs`, the coordinator wrappers in `packages/transaction/src/state_abi.rs:149-170`, and the six unused deferred owners where finding 127 locates them in `packages/vectors/src/maturity_first_party.rs`; their consolidation is unrelated API work. Finding 128's private observed-layer comparison remains reusable within vectors without promoting that module (`packages/vectors/src/lib.rs`).

79. The route to accepted announcement bytes is the constant-elision witness ratified at (`[ADR025-dec:relay-witness:constant-elision]`): the witness carries the predecessor metadata's variable region and the executing leaf restores its constant regions before the existing checks, preserving the canonical commitment; the frontend classifies layout rows as constant or variable without learning a target limit, because those classes describe the encoding itself; the backend takes the target's argument-width policy as an emission input and refuses a non-contiguous variable layout or an over-wide carried schedule, with the linker re-measuring admission widths separately from execution bounds, because an inadmissible new-deployment program must be refused before emission; one 25-byte header is a width-checked linker symbol and the eight reserved zeros are a leaf literal, because the linker already carries constants and successor copy-through already synthesizes those zeros (`packages/realization/src/state_codec.rs:132-155`, `packages/tapscript/src/state_announcement.rs:391-446,516-532`, `packages/linker/src/tests/state_symbol_tests.rs:190-233`); the historical whole-item schedule remains replay-only, preserving the refused run's exact bytes without offering that schedule for deployment; the route occupies `T11-100` through `T11-109` and refines only ruling 70's route clause, leaving Wave 9's node-free scope unchanged because its continuity work remains useful before acceptance; accepted evidence requires a newly funded predecessor under the changed leaf and a fresh second immutable four-file corpus through the existing executor and schema 8, because the changed static tree cannot reuse the old funded predecessor, with the fee-free environment disclosed and no artifact promoted by acceptance alone (`packages/linker/src/state_bundle.rs:373-409`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_corpus.rs:37-55,1040-1067`, `packages/target-elements-conformance/src/protocol.rs:229,3208-3225`, `scripts/elements-native-executor.py:2199-2200`). The fresh run proceeds when its prerequisite bites are green; `T11-098` now depends on the accepted evidence and validated continuity comparison supplied by `T11-108`.

### Wave-10 rulings · `rule:phase6:wave10-rulings`

The Wave-10 study's nine decisions and two open questions are ruled here on the merits as rulings 80 through 90, open to revision at the candidate audit like every ruling on this card. They are taken over that study rather than over the task text alone at (`task:guide14-exec:wave10`): the guide assigns deliverables to waves and never backlog rows, so the mapping onto four gated bites is this record's inference. The study also locates the structural prerequisite for those bites: thirty matrix rows declare no evidence boundary, and `classify` returns a typed non-answer before it can consult a report, so the producers cannot answer those rows until their boundaries are minted (`plans/drafts/wave10-root-history-and-recovery-study.md`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_safety.rs:3806-3817`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_evidence.rs:2065-2075`).

80. The vectors crate owns the typed `StateRootEdge` beside exact-byte continuity projection under `T11-114`: its STATE outpoints use `Outpoint`, its operation uses `OperationId::AnnounceMaturity`, and its new `MaturityTransitionCertificate` value carries the existing `ProjectionId::TransitionCertificate` identifier rather than inventing another identifier. The architecture already declares that projection for the operation and the compiler requires it; the transaction view checks only the relation available from its entries and leaves chain freshness as a residual, while the linker owns construction recipes rather than the verdict (`packages/transaction/src/bytes.rs:541`, `packages/transaction/src/state_view.rs:5-14,55-66,84-92`, `packages/architecture/src/ids.rs:100-113,298-306`, `packages/architecture/src/spec.rs:649-655,1662`, `packages/compiler/src/maturity_announcement_plan.rs:57-61`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_continuity_report.rs:49-63`).

81. `validate_state_root_history` under `T11-114` applies §17.1's six clauses, §17.3's existing `state_bundle_continuity` inequality and the operation's root and cardinality declarations to an ordered edge sequence. The sixteen §16.8 faults are host-decidable against the constructed sequence; the three-edge invalid-intermediate case must refuse even when its final cursor equals the honest successor, because §14.6 rejects final-cursor equality as a substitute for validating every edge. `stale-predecessor` and `wrong-current-root-view` are answered against the stated structural cursor, while their possible chain-current reading remains an explicit residual under ruling 89 (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:2246-2261,2716-2729`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_continuity.rs:595-604`, `packages/architecture/src/spec.rs:619-622,1612-1647,2085-2093`, `packages/linker/src/state_bundle.rs:375-410`, `packages/transaction/src/state_view.rs:221-233`).

82. `MaturityRootCheckpoint` under `T11-115` pairs the caller-stated `BranchContext` with §17.2's ten bound facts: eight carried or derived from admitted and public bytes, and the linked candidate and candidate ABI carried as declarations with typed provenance. A modelled rewind and reprojection stale an observation when its transaction is removed or changed and refuse silent reuse across another prefix. This establishes the stated checkpoint binding over a modelled branch, while the branch's external establishment and actual chain observation remain residuals; the tree already uses opaque model checkpoints and typed reorganization refusals on that distinction (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:2731-2748`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_corpus.rs:138-155,395-412`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_native.rs:176-214`, `packages/transaction/src/operator_right.rs:23-58,432,510,795`, `packages/model/src/ledger.rs:749-759,1790-1809`).

83. `T11-115` gives the root-history registry, landed on `0.6.259-dev` as `MaturityMutationRegistry` (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_evidence.rs:613-661`), a construction path deriving the sixteen §16.8 mutations through a `registered` constructor that carries no visibility modifier and is therefore private to its module (`:622`), makes its standing follow the mutation content, and retires `BranchIndexedHistoryIsNotProjectable` rather than narrowing it. The matrix already gives those rows `RootHistoryEdge` locators, and the registry already has typed section, row and locator entries; its current constant outstanding verdict and constructor-free empty state describe the missing producer rather than a chain-freshness question. The sequence validator owns structural mutations, while chain freshness remains a report residual (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_safety.rs:645-663,2351-2494`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_evidence.rs:504-655`, `packages/vectors/src/subject.rs:56-71`).

84. `T11-116` makes the process boundary a typed `PublicAnnouncementHandoff` containing §14.7's public inputs and a `recover_public_successor` function consuming that value alone, with an integration binary whose imports exclude the linker bundle and planner. A type and pinned import list constrain this process less strongly than a separate crate boundary, so that limit stays visible in the third exit. The handoff carries published deployment parameters needed for recovery and excludes the creator's retained instance, deployment identity, descriptor and path; §16.13 names process memory, a temporary file and a wallet descriptor as the private dependencies the recovery claim must exclude (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:2263-2279,2706-2708`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_continuity.rs:671-694`, `packages/linker/src/state_bundle.rs:354-357,501-514`, `packages/transaction/src/live_handoff.rs:1-24`, `packages/transaction/src/state_view.rs:5-9`).

85. The §14.7 report's eleven items have ten existing producer paths from public inputs, while absence of creator-private dependencies is the eleventh claim and requires the typed handoff's construction proof under `T11-116`. Witness metadata, nonce, static root, linked leaf and control data support reconstructing the successor program and comparing it with output 0 of the same transaction; equality establishes that transaction's public-byte reconstructibility, with no inference about freshness or origin. The retained-context path instead requires the creator's bundle and identity and is excluded from recovery; §1.14 and §23 keep the recovery result separate from the other evidence roles (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:414-430,2263-2279`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_continuity.rs:591-621,637-694,811-851,1542-1590,1767-1775`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_native.rs:176-214`, `packages/linker/src/state_bundle.rs:501-514`).

86. Under `T11-116` and `T11-117`, nine §16.13 faults are one-axis mutations refused by the constrained recovery process and four are construction proofs over what its handoff and function cannot name. Such a refusal answers its declared row because those rows have no target refusing layer, name the published announcement as their control and name the report as their carrier; §14.7 defines that report's constrained producer. The continuity mutants have distinct declared target layers and acquire no standing by analogy. The two reports and boundaries in `T11-117` give the root-history and public-recovery evidence classes their separate reachable producers (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:2157-2172,2263-2279`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_safety.rs:331-333,414-415,1284-1289,3219-3343`, `packages/vectors/src/matrix.rs:220-265`, `plans/drafts/accepted-byte-classification-study.md:95-106`).

87. Both reports under `T11-117` carry three typed synthetic-origin residuals: no chain observation, no root-cursor freshness and no origin of the accepted archive beyond admission of its pinned bytes. The continuity report already validates a caller-stated-branch residual separately; keeping these limits in each report's validated bytes prevents a structural edge check or public-byte equality from reading as chain, freshness or predecessor-provenance evidence (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_continuity_report.rs:86-116,1397-1404`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_corpus.rs:193-205`, `packages/transaction/src/state_view.rs:221-233`).

88. §16.1's last positive case remains one row under `T11-117`: the guide states one numbered conjunction of semantic, constructor, root and recovery projections, and the matrix transcribes fourteen positive cases. Splitting the conjunction would change the 206-row denominator and answer a different question from the guide's. Its projection terms remain distinguishable within that row; the acceptance boundary remains its blocker, owned by the accepted-byte classification study's first open question rather than by either new report (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:2443-2456`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_safety.rs:674-691,1460-1474,3559-3565`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_evidence.rs:3340-3363`, `plans/drafts/accepted-byte-classification-study.md:182`).

89. §16.8's `stale-predecessor` and `wrong-current-root-view` take §17.1's structural cursor reading for the validator under `T11-114` and its report under `T11-117`: both are edge-sequence rows, and clause 1 compares the predecessor with the cursor before the edge. A chain-current reading remains a typed freshness residual because §17.2 uses staleness for a reorganization that changes or removes the transaction. Guide evidence that these two rows require a live chain-current verdict rather than §17.1's stated-cursor verdict revises this ruling at the candidate audit; a structural refusal alone claims no chain observation (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:2580-2584,2716-2748`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_safety.rs:2351-2374`, `packages/transaction/src/state_view.rs:221-233`).

90. `T11-117` gives root history and public recovery separate `EvidenceBoundary` members, matching §1.14's separate evidence classes and routing each report to its own standing. One shared member whose standing is chosen from the row's section would implement one routing rule in two places, the wrong-boundary failure the current classifier documents. The guide's boundary vocabulary remains the candidate-audit check on this two-member reading (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:414-430`, `packages/vectors/src/matrix.rs:220-265`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_evidence.rs:1241-1249`).

### Wave-11 rulings · `rule:phase6:wave11-rulings`

The Wave-11 study's eight decisions and three open questions are ruled here on the merits as rulings 91 through 97, open to revision at the candidate audit like every ruling on this card. They are taken over that study rather than over the task text alone at (`task:guide14-exec:wave11`): the guide assigns deliverables to waves and never backlog rows, so the mapping onto four gated bites and three conditional bites is this record's inference. The structural boundary is that the tree builds no sponsored STATE candidate, construction refuses both sponsored forms by `SponsoredMaturityFormHasNoCarrier` under ruling 58 at (`rule:phase6:wave7-rulings`), and the register gives Wave 11 no owned row; acceptance, refusal and explicit unsupported status therefore account for its four forms. OQ1 retains the guide's reading of explicit unsupported status, while OQ2 and OQ3 retain the §16 matrix's report-layer and selected-policy readings (`plans/drafts/wave11-sponsored-forms-study.md`, `plans/guides/guide_fourteen_conceptual_register.md`, `packages/transaction/src/state_construct.rs`).

91. Wave 11's four forms are `RequestedForm` crossed with `SponsorChangeRequest`. The sponsorless form without change has the admitted archive's acceptance; sponsorless with change is refused as `SponsorChangeWithoutSponsoredForm` by the request constructor; sponsored without and with change are refused at construction step 1 as `SponsoredMaturityFormHasNoCarrier`, an explicit unsupported status naming the absent carrier of a well-formed request. The sponsored control at this opening is the sponsored request over the sponsorless candidate's view and ABI, refused at construction, and change present has no supported sponsored instance in this generation. `T11-129` pins all four dispositions, including the sponsored-with-change case that the current tests only construct as a request (`packages/transaction/src/state_request.rs`, `packages/transaction/src/state_construct.rs`, `packages/transaction/src/error.rs`, `packages/transaction/src/tests/state_construct_tests.rs`, `packages/transaction/src/tests/state_request_tests.rs`).

92. Wave 11 opens before `T11-064`, whose own gate exercises a composed observation in Phase 7. Ruling 58's leaf premise stands, while its model premise is narrower: ADR-024 says the model describes a single-operation transaction before the refit, and the realization already evaluates and refuses faults in a sponsored announcement observation. The sponsored form's defining membership and multiplicity negatives still depend on relations `T11-064` re-scopes or retires; a sponsored positive without their boundary would lack the refusals that give its acceptance meaning. C1 therefore requires `T11-064` and a ruling revisiting ruling 58, and C2 requires C1 and a provisioned node; neither is a Wave-11 opening bite (`adr/024-closure-exact-linking.md`, `packages/realization/src/tests/open_flow_tests.rs`, `packages/realization/src/evaluate.rs`, `plans/backlog.md`, `packages/linker/src/state_carrier.rs`).

93. All twelve §16.10 rows under `SponsorArmAwaitsRegionScoping` remain typed non-answers at this opening. Under `T11-132`, nine whose declared boundary a sponsored builder could reach take `SponsoredControlRefusedAtConstruction`, as do the two §16.1 sponsored positives now described by an acceptance reason; `two-sponsor-envelopes`, `sponsor-member-unclassified`, and `zero-valued-sponsor-member-under-exact-role-structure` retain a narrowed `SponsorArmAwaitsRegionScoping` reason. The request, construction, finalization and signing refusals of a sponsorless form do not answer a mutation of a sponsored control. C3 depends on C1 for the eleven construction-refused rows and on `T11-064` for the three relation questions (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_safety.rs`, `packages/transaction/src/state_finalize.rs`, `packages/transaction/src/error.rs`, `plans/backlog.md`).

94. The second exit rests on structural exclusion of sponsor amounts from the request, realization and four canonical reports; `T11-131` pins that exclusion at each renderer's key boundary using the live generation's public forbidden-key vocabulary and a staged-key test. A scan for an individual sponsor amount in bytes from the sponsorless archive would have no supplied amount to test. The two `report-publishes-*` rows remain `ReportLayerRequired` alongside thirteen other report-layer rows: the canonical safety report is assembled from the classified plan, so its validation cannot supply the independent producer that classifies those rows (`packages/transaction/src/state_construct.rs`, `packages/transaction/src/state_finalize.rs`, `packages/realization/src/tests/open_flow_tests.rs`, `packages/vectors/src/live_minimality_report.rs`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_evidence.rs`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_report.rs`).

95. Sponsored acceptances, the target verdict on missing sponsor authorization, the target half of balanced STATE corruption, and exact target and policy verdicts require a sponsored candidate and an Elements-provisioned compute node; the nodes in service lack that toolchain. Host refusals, model relations and report-key exclusion have host boundaries. The operator's default hash type covers every transaction input and output, so adding a sponsor member to accepted sponsorless bytes invalidates operator authorization rather than answering the sponsor row. C2 carries the candidate, funding and signing steps, `expected_layer` subjects, driver census and corpus admission; compute-node provisioning is its separate condition, and positive-fee-floor relayability belongs to deployment or Wave 12 (`packages/transaction/src/operator_signing.rs`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_native.rs`, `scripts/live-native-maturity-capture.sh`, `plans/phases/06-state-and-maturity.md`).

96. The first Wave-11 exit is CONDITIONALLY MET on a STATE transition that reads no sponsor and on the model comparison `T11-130` pins, with the target half conditional on C1 and C2 because no sponsored byte exists. The second exit is MET structurally and receives `T11-131`'s key tests. The third is MET once `T11-129` pins the fourth form and `T11-132` gives the two sponsored positives and twelve sponsor rows exact reasons; §23's eighth conjunct follows that disposition. The balanced STATE corruption fails the retained STATE-side leaf checks and per-asset conservation independently of sponsor checks; its model comparison is exit evidence rather than a target-row answer. Wave 7's second exit retains its landed CONDITIONALLY MET status (`packages/transaction/src/state_construct.rs`, `plans/phases/06-state-and-maturity.md`, `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md`, `packages/realization/src/tests/open_flow_tests.rs`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_safety.rs`).

97. `T11-129`, `T11-130` and `T11-131` depend on the opening record alone; `T11-132` follows `T11-129` because its reason cites that refusal test, and follows landed `T11-125` where the same comments stand. `T11-133` receives each gate's evidence and restates the acceptance-awaiting count moved by bite 4. C1, C2 and C3 retain the conditions in rulings 92, 93 and 95 and receive rows when those conditions hold; the four present bites add no sponsored byte, node verdict, answered sponsor row or census change (`plans/drafts/wave11-sponsored-forms-study.md`, `plans/backlog.md`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_safety.rs`).

### Wave-12 rulings · `rule:phase6:wave12-rulings`

The Wave-12 study's nine decisions and three open questions are ruled here on the merits as rulings 98 through 104, open to revision at the candidate audit like every ruling on this card. They are taken over that study rather than over the task text alone at (`task:guide14-exec:wave12`): the guide assigns deliverables to waves and never backlog rows, so the mapping onto five gated bites and two conditional bites is this record's inference. Both maturity runs of record carry the node's own transaction weight in their recorded responses, admitted by the corpus and compared by no module; the first exit therefore has a host-side comparison over that one observed dimension in bite 2, while every other dimension retains a named absence. The register assigns `G14C-03` to Wave 12, whose remaining bounded-corpus clause receives bite 4's evidence (`plans/drafts/wave12-resource-study.md`, `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md`, `plans/guides/guide_fourteen_conceptual_register.md`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_corpus.rs:609`).

98. Wave 12 uses §18.2's fourteen cases, §14.8's twenty-one fields in guide order and the target roster's eleven dimensions, with predicted and observed figures or typed reasons for their absence. `T11-136` measures the two archived submissions and `T11-137` adds minimum- and maximum-lead candidates over each archive's recorded funding; the two sponsored cases retain Wave 11's construction refusal, and a candidate maximum sponsor shape has no subject in this generation. Maxima are identified separately, with within-schedule ties stated because one transaction does not maximize every objective (`rule:guide14-exec:measurements`, `rule:guide14-exec:resource-objectives`, `packages/linker/src/state_resource.rs:325–348`, `packages/target-elements/src/resource.rs:30–71`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_native.rs:805–813`, `plans/phases/06-state-and-maturity.md:1190`).

99. The first exit compares target observation with a prediction from the same exact candidate bytes wherever the target supplied a figure. The two archived responses supply transaction weight, admitted through `ValidatedMaturityCorpus::exchanges`; `T11-136` compares each with the host decode, types other observations by their actual boundary and tests a synthetic mismatch carrying both figures. `ResourceProjectionDisagreement` compares two host figures and is neither that target comparison nor its negative. N1 widens observations of added leads under its compute-node condition without conditioning this exit (`rule:guide14-exec:resource-comparison`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_corpus.rs:609`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_corpus.rs:1356–1371`, `scripts/elements-native-executor.py:4627–4664`, `packages/linker/src/tests/state_resource_tests.rs:766–769`).

100. The nonce-search corpus is the eight real-curve predecessor and successor derivations performed by the measured candidates under the reviewed 4,096-attempt budget. `T11-138` reports each selected nonce, attempt count and independently checked leastness, the greatest attempt count and the landed exhaustion residual; the closure under `T11-140` applies this closing sentence to `G14C-03`: "Wave 12 reports the bounded corpus — eight named real-curve derivations under the reviewed 4,096-attempt budget, each checked least by an independent scan, whose greatest selection took the attempt count its corpus test pins — beside the landed residual that a later admissible nonce may exist beyond the budget, as host evidence over a finite corpus and not as a claim that every metadata admits a nonce within it" (`packages/tapscript/src/state_constructor.rs:523–549`, `packages/tapscript/src/state_constructor.rs:773–786`, `plans/phases/06-state-and-maturity.md:710`, `plans/guides/guide_fourteen_conceptual_register.md:111`).

101. A missing measurement has a typed absence wherever Wave 12 reads or renders a figure, while a measured zero whose reason is the reviewed contract has its own standing. `T11-135` makes the linker's projection refusal carry `checked: Option<u64>` and refuse an unmeasured projection with `None`; `T11-136` and `T11-139` preserve reasons through comparison and rendering. The zero-bound default in `decoded_witness_metadata` is a fail-closed policy bound owned by the continuity projection, not a measurement under the third exit (`packages/linker/src/state_resource.rs:730–743`, `packages/linker/src/state_error.rs:396–403`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_continuity.rs:651–658`).

102. `T11-139` keeps the noncanonical timing report in a diagnostics type its canonical renderer never reads: it carries host construction time, nonce attempts and each archived run's sidecar status with duration absent because no archived artifact carries one. N2 carries target execution durations under its compute-node condition. The explicit non-calibration result is one variant spelling §18.1's sentence beside its seven candidate parameters, with a test pinning the rendered result (`rule:guide14-exec:candidate-parameters`, `packages/vectors/src/live_resource_report.rs:86–119`, `packages/vectors/src/live_resource_report.rs:391–436`, `scripts/live-native-maturity-capture.sh:64–69`, `scripts/live-native-maturity-capture.sh:242–281`).

103. The second exit rests on `T11-139`'s rendered census of every absence reason, each local to the missing subject or tied to the upstream interpreter limit, and on its source test excluding stale blocker vocabulary. `OwnerSighashNotComputable` and `NoAcceptingControlExists` do not explain maturity omissions; `EG-011` explains interpreter figures only, and the compute-node condition explains only observations of candidates no recorded run judged (`packages/vectors/src/live_evidence.rs:145–217`, `packages/vectors/src/live_resource_report.rs:886–889`, `plans/reference/upstream-frictions.md:107`, `plans/phases/06-state-and-maturity.md:1176`).

104. `T11-135` and `T11-136` each follow the opening record, `T11-137` follows `T11-136`, `T11-138` follows `T11-137`, and `T11-139` receives all four; `T11-140` alone records their closure on gate evidence and changes the register cell. N1, target observations of added leads, and N2, archived execution durations, retain the condition of an Elements-provisioned compute node and receive rows when that condition holds. Positive-fee-floor relayability requires a fee-paying form under Wave 11's C1 and a deployment policy, because the sponsorless candidate declares no fee (`plans/phases/06-state-and-maturity.md:952`, `packages/transaction/src/state_finalize.rs:155–156`, `plans/phases/06-state-and-maturity.md:1218`).

### Wave-2 plan findings · `sec:phase6:wave2-findings`

Wave 2 needs a bespoke validated announcement container while sharing the generic row vocabulary in `packages/compiler/src/operation_plan.rs`: that module's target relation, case, carrier, coverage and lifecycle rows are already reusable, but its `ValidatedTargetOperationPlan` and planning entry point are explicitly compact-specific.

The compiler's announce-maturity Authorization/Operation and Constructibility/Operation relations are external obligations, not shape-proved obligations. The operator capability already exists in `packages/compiler/src/capability.rs`, but `packages/compiler/src/proof.rs`, `packages/compiler/src/source.rs` and `packages/compiler/src/coverage.rs` do not yet carry that classification through the plan, so the operator-evidence sweep (`T11-013`) precedes any plan publication.

Under ruling 13 the public-fact census becomes fifteen: six input-side and six output-side STATE fields, the requested cycle, and the two lead facts (`packages/realization/src/identity.rs`, `packages/realization/src/declarations/announce_maturity.rs`). The symbolic laws relating the sides live in the requirement layer (`packages/compiler/src/maturity_announcement_requirements.rs`), while the executable census remains 26 relations and 23 edges until a later lowering bite because disclosure nodes are not relations (`packages/realization/src/declassification.rs`, `packages/realization/src/tests/announce_maturity_tests.rs`).

Under rulings 11 and 13, the combined refit bite `T11-021` for architecture-owned lead bounds and side-indexed STATE keys landed in `0.6.152-dev`; the altered-lead-bounds corruption check in B5 (`T11-017`) now waits on B4 (`T11-016`) alone because `packages/compiler/src/maturity_announcement_requirements.rs` can consume both keyed bounds and keyed successor laws. B4, B6, and B5 landed in `0.6.153-dev`, `0.6.154-dev`, and `0.6.155-dev`, and B7 landed in `0.6.156-dev`.

The output position is an abstract role in the announcement plan, with its concrete index owned downstream. This follows the existing boundary in `packages/compiler/src/operation_plan.rs`, whose abstract carrier and coverage rows deliberately contain no transaction position or target encoding.

No new digest enters the announcement plan, and the existing `ArchitectureBinding` in the typed `TargetOperationSource` remains the architecture binding. `packages/compiler/src/operation_plan.rs` expressly reserves no digest field, so the announcement container has no basis to mint one.

The six typed STATE lifecycle exits — `AdmitDeposits`, `Cycle`, `Redeem`, `ReceiptRelabel`, `Clear` and `AnnounceMaturity` — are published by the declaration and architecture censuses in `packages/realization/src/declarations/announce_maturity.rs` and `packages/architecture/src/spec.rs`. `OperationId::AnnounceMaturity` already exists in `packages/architecture/src/ids.rs`, so Wave 2 invents no further operation identifier.

The three empty canonical-delta negative classes remain in the corruption catalogue as derived negatives in `0.6.155-dev`; no focused runtime witness applies because the announce-maturity declaration's expected set is empty (`packages/architecture/src/spec.rs`, `packages/realization/src/validate.rs`) and duplicate partition references are refused during observation normalization, before the relation verdict. The catalogue pins them as derived rows, and the validator's documentation states that ground.

### Wave-3 plan findings · `sec:phase6:wave3-findings`

The Wave-3 opening study was a read-only source study: it ran no gate and self-audited 283 file citations.

Deliverable 1, exact target capability assessment — PARTIAL. Generic mapping, compact assessment and live-transfer patterns exist, but the studied tree had no maturity assessment consuming the validated announcement projection or classifying all eighteen guide groups, and the two generic sighash prerequisites remained Incomplete (`packages/tapscript/src/capability.rs:497–716`; `packages/tapscript/src/operation_assessment.rs:368–394,444–550`; `packages/tapscript/src/lib.rs:4–21`; `packages/target-elements/src/capability.rs:723–737`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:1284–1318`).

Deliverable 2, operator-key encoding closure — PARTIAL. Approved x-only width, owner-shape validation, a curve seam and an independent point oracle exist, but deployment-bound operator identity and its closure path do not, and width validation alone is insufficient (`packages/target-elements/src/encoding.rs:333`; `packages/tapscript/src/live_constructor.rs:291–346`; `packages/transaction/src/live_taproot.rs:91–104`; `packages/target-elements-conformance/src/owner_key_oracle.rs:117–144`; `packages/architecture/src/deployment.rs:175–191`).

Deliverable 3, selected operator profile — PARTIAL. A source-owned, revision-bound owner profile exists, but its receipt/owner protected-data map is not a STATE/operator map and its descriptive linker wrapper is publicly constructible (`packages/tapscript/src/authorization.rs:319–322,471–496,694–805`; `packages/linker/src/live_symbol.rs:492–528`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:958–975,1393–1402`).

Deliverable 4, independent message recomputation — PARTIAL. The owner census and Elements message implementation include spent fields and output witnesses, but an operator candidate still needs a validated generic census and established metadata, leaf and deployment binding (`packages/transaction/src/live_census.rs:1099–1205,1515–1577`; `packages/transaction/src/live_message.rs:183–318`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:2070–2092`).

Deliverable 5, target-generic public-test signer capability where needed — ABSENT from the native signing API. The closed operation subject has sponsor signing only, whose ECDSA Segwit-v0 implementation cannot produce the required script-path Schnorr witness (`packages/target-elements-conformance/src/protocol.rs:2191–2201,2515–2557`; `scripts/elements-native-executor.py:4509–4533`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:1343–1391`).

Deliverable 6, strict response shape — PARTIAL. Framing, schema/case checks and sponsor success/refusal shape exist, but no operator result binds signer, profile, input, leaf and exact candidate, while sponsor acceptance requires only a nonempty outer witness and a present echo (`packages/target-elements-conformance/src/executor.rs:1982–2041`; `packages/target-elements-conformance/src/protocol.rs:2784–2845,3073–3083`; `packages/transaction/src/live_signing.rs:268–330`).

Deliverable 7, target-native operator-signature control — ABSENT from the inspected operator surface. Existing positive/readback recomputation and six controls concern live owners, and the native subject registry has no operator-capable script-path signer (`packages/vectors/src/live_owner_observation.rs:151–220,1035–1079`; `packages/target-elements-conformance/src/protocol.rs:2515–2557`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:1393–1402`).

Deliverable 8, unknown-key negatives — PARTIAL. The target contract explicitly allows unknown nonempty key types to succeed without verification and owner negative obligations exist, but operator-specific admission and target-boundary controls for all seven key faults are missing (`packages/target-elements/src/authorization.rs:426–440,929–960`; `packages/tapscript/src/authorization.rs:842–860`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:1333–1341`).

Deliverable 9, protocol revision update on both sides if required — PARTIAL. Both implementations and the agreement test use revision 7; the proposed new signing record requires a coordinated next revision, while a wholly in-process solution would not (`packages/target-elements-conformance/src/protocol.rs:218`; `scripts/elements-native-executor.py:510`; `scripts/test-executor-boundaries.py:810–818`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:3675–3677`).

Exit 1, one finalized synthetic candidate signed by the public test operator — ABSENT as an established operator result. Public owner signing and synthetic owner observations are precedents, but no operator-capable native subject or result exists (`packages/target-elements-conformance/src/test_material.rs:143–171`; `packages/vectors/src/live_owner_observation.rs:151–220`; `packages/target-elements-conformance/src/protocol.rs:2515–2557`).

Exit 2, returned witness binds exact bytes — PARTIAL. Existing owner census/response and sponsor echo checks provide components, but no operator result combines exact candidate, census, leaf and deployment binding with independent verification and observed target acceptance (`packages/transaction/src/live_census.rs:1114–1205`; `packages/transaction/src/live_signing.rs:268–296`; `packages/target-elements-conformance/src/protocol.rs:2825–2842`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:2094–2106`).

Exit 3, no production key interface exists — PARTIAL as a positive closure claim. The native operation subjects expose no production scalar or key-import field and sponsor signing uses fixed disposable material, but the public in-process test-material constructor accepts an arbitrary in-range scalar and cannot establish its provenance, so a fixed-handle operator API and exclusion tests remain necessary (`packages/target-elements-conformance/src/protocol.rs:2515–2557`; `scripts/elements-native-executor.py:641–647,4157–4175`; `packages/target-elements-conformance/src/test_material.rs:137–171`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:532–550`).

Exit 4, no caller-authored profile accepted as evidence — PARTIAL. Owner signing requires a private reviewed establishment witness and checks its revision, but no operator counterpart exists and neither the wire selector nor a publicly paired linker disposition can become that counterpart by assertion (`packages/transaction/src/live_handoff.rs:181–199,226–264`; `packages/tapscript/src/authorization.rs:471–496`; `packages/linker/src/live_symbol.rs:499–527`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:1393–1402`).

The conceptual register adds one OPEN Wave-3 obligation beyond the nine deliverables and four original exits: `G14C-05` requires an affine right and a non-equivocation record, which neither cloneable owner signing state nor per-exchange duplicate-case rejection discharges (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen_conceptual_register.md:7–9,57–61,85,118`; `packages/transaction/src/live_handoff.rs:200–203`; `packages/target-elements-conformance/src/executor.rs:2050–2118`).

The model remains the semantic ground truth: its abstract operator membership check precedes maturity checks, while realization keeps operator relations evidence-required. Target-side reproduction must authenticate the deployment operator key, freeze the candidate and census, recompute the selected message, verify the returned signature and exact bindings, observe the committed-key leaf and protected-term controls, then map that verified deployment authority to the model's abstract operator in vectors/conformance without adding an identity to realization (`packages/model/src/ops/maturity.rs:31–79`; `packages/model/src/signer.rs:17–29`; `packages/model/src/conformance.rs:94–119`; `packages/realization/src/evaluate.rs:159–203,309–319,429–433`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:319–339,1393–1402,2365–2373`).

The nine planned bites are exact assessment (`T11-024`), operator encoding and profile (`T11-025`), candidate deployment binding (`T11-026`), generic census and operator handoff (`T11-027`), atomic native protocol and public signer (`T11-028`), adversarial response boundary (`T11-029`), affine right and non-equivocation (`T11-030`), synthetic operator/native evidence (`T11-031`), and closure and handoff (`T11-032`); only `T11-024` needed no decision at the opening boundary.

14. The gate of record in `scripts/ci.sh` has no rustdoc lane, so private and unresolved intra-doc links survived green gates until warnings-as-errors documentation gates exposed and repaired three in `packages/target-elements-conformance/src/protocol.rs`, `packages/vectors/src/live_evidence.rs`, and `packages/vectors/src/live_private_restart.rs`; adding a workspace rustdoc lane under warnings as errors belongs to the closure sweep (`T11-032`).

15. The Elements functional-test framework's transaction decoder leaves witness vectors empty when finalized bytes omit the witness section, while the consensus message hashes the witness census at every input and output position; `scripts/elements-native-executor.py` completes the in-memory census before hashing and echoes the original bytes, so confirming that message equals the target's belongs to the native evidence sweep (`T11-031`).

16. The genesis that `scripts/elements-native-executor.py` hashes is the observed session genesis read little-endian into the framework's integer serializer, preserving the octets that `packages/transaction/src/script_path_signing.rs` appends; equality is asserted only against a stub framework, so the native capture must confirm it together with the grown-vector member selected by `packages/transaction/src/operator_signing.rs` (`T11-031`).

17. The first native operator run funded the predecessor, obtained both signatures and saw all six submission controls refused at their declared script-path boundary, yet the positive received the same "Invalid Schnorr signature" verdict after the carrier's independent verifier had accepted it; the node-free gates had passed because the fixture and adapter agreed on the same printed genesis order and their repeated-byte fixtures could not distinguish it from internal order. Target-framework recomputation isolated the deployment genesis, and `0.6.167-dev` now applies one byte-order rule at the three conversion or binding sites: the fixture reverses the printed identity before the kernel in `packages/vectors/src/maturity_operator.rs`, the adapter reads printed octets big-endian for the target's internal serializer in `scripts/elements-native-executor.py`, and the handoff compares reversed printed bytes with the internal deployment in `packages/transaction/src/operator_signing.rs`; non-symmetric-genesis regressions in `packages/transaction/src/tests/operator_signing_tests.rs` and `packages/vectors/src/maturity_operator.rs` pin both orders, and `T11-032` owns the closure record.

18. The revision-8 live-transfer capture pair predates protocol revision 8: `scripts/live-native-v2-r8-capture.sh` prints protocol revision 7 and `scripts/test-live-native-v2-r8-capture.sh` checks that value, because `r8` names the capture revision rather than the native protocol revision; `T11-032` owns recording that distinction or renaming the pair so the two revision namespaces cannot be mistaken.

19. The hash-citation families table had no rule for the suite-provenance fields carried by the operator corpus, and the shared capture writer's capability rendering predated the revision-8 script-path signing capability; `0.6.168-dev` repairs the writer census in `packages/vectors/tests/common/mod.rs`, `0.6.169-dev` repairs the provenance family in `lint/hash-citation-families.tsv`, and `T11-032` owns carrying both repairs into the closure account.

20. The shared writer in `packages/vectors/tests/common/mod.rs` retains no request bytes for funding steps, and its closed ceremony census requires a new carrier to reuse the generic `Report` ceremony and its live-transfer Rust test name, as the operator carrier does in `packages/vectors/tests/maturity_operator.rs`; the strict importer consequently reconstructs funding subjects from retained issuance and coin facts in `packages/vectors/src/live_corpus_native_operator.rs`, and `T11-032` owns deciding whether the writer should retain those bytes and give new carriers their own ceremony and test identity.

21. The realization crate leaves operator authorization external in `packages/realization/src/evaluate.rs`, while `packages/realization/src/observation.rs` carries no expected operator identity and exposes no decision over an observed authorization; the admitted record therefore answers ten of eleven operator-evidence rows but leaves membership as the external evidence requirement with twelve STATE facts unconstructed in `packages/vectors/src/maturity_operator.rs`. `T11-032` owns naming the evaluation that decides membership before that row can move from external evidence.

Wave 3 closed in its planned nine-bite order: exact assessment (`T11-024`, `0.6.158-dev`); operator encoding and profile (`T11-025`, `0.6.160-dev`); candidate deployment binding (`T11-026`, `0.6.161-dev`); generic census and operator handoff (`T11-027`, `0.6.162-dev`, with three commits under the tag); atomic native protocol and public signer (`T11-028`, `0.6.164-dev`); adversarial response boundary (`T11-029`, `0.6.165-dev`); affine right and non-equivocation (`T11-030`, `0.6.163-dev`); synthetic operator and native evidence (`T11-031`, `0.6.166-dev`, `0.6.167-dev`, `0.6.168-dev`, `0.6.169-dev`); and closure and handoff (`T11-032`, `0.6.170-dev`, `0.6.171-dev`).

The native verdict is one target-accepted operator positive whose mined readback is byte-identical, with wrong key, wrong candidate, wrong leaf, signature width, signature type, and protected term all refused at the script-path layer with the node's detail; unknown-key admission, duplicate authorization before any signing request, and signature verification are first-party facts. Operator membership remains the one external evidence requirement, and its twelve input/output STATE facts remain unconstructed because realization exposes no decision over an observed authorization (`0.6.169-dev`).

Findings 14–19 are disposed on their evidence: 14 is closed by the warnings-as-errors workspace rustdoc lane (`0.6.170-dev`); 15's grown witness census and target message agreement are confirmed by the accepted native run; 16's genesis order and the failed-positive diagnosis recorded in 17 are repaired by the three-site byte-order rule and the subsequent accepted run (`0.6.167-dev`); 18 is recorded here as a namespace distinction, because `r8` in `scripts/live-native-v2-r8-capture.sh` names capture revision 8 while that preserved corpus still uses native protocol revision 7, whereas the operator corpus uses native protocol revision 8; and 19's writer capability and suite-provenance families are repaired in `0.6.168-dev` and `0.6.169-dev`.

Findings 20 and 21 pass onward under rulings 31 and 33 at (`rule:phase6:wave4-rulings`): the shared writer retains funding request bytes in Wave 4 while a constructor-specific ceremony and Rust test identity wait for an actual constructor carrier in Wave 8; realization gains its fail-closed membership seam in Wave 4, Wave 5 produces the authenticated membership witness, and Wave 7 binds it to finalized observation bytes; the constructor exposes the twelve STATE facts as typed slice commitments in Wave 4 and the ABI carries their observation in Wave 7; selected-profile closure does not promote the generic `Incomplete` capability entries, and the Wave-7 ABI remains responsible for binding the finalized target bytes and witness roles.

### Wave-4 plan findings · `sec:phase6:wave4-findings`

The Wave-4 opening study was a read-only source study with no gate run, about one hundred and five timed steps, and every citation self-audited against file lengths.

Deliverable 1, metadata commitment leaf — PARTIAL. Wave 1 fixes a big-endian `u32` nonce and the prototype searches from zero, but no production normalizer, normative bound, exact zero-bound convention, exhaustion evidence, or host-leastness record exists (`packages/realization/src/state_codec.rs:27-53`; `packages/realization/src/state_codec.rs:130-155`; `packages/target-elements-conformance/src/constructor/canonical.rs:151-198`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen_conceptual_register.md:87`).

Prototype and transaction code derive version/parity/key/path blocks and linker has generic control recipes, but no `StateControlRecipe` or `StateLeafRole` binds the outer metadata sibling, static path, and exact executing role (`packages/target-elements-conformance/src/constructor/tree.rs:459-490`; `packages/transaction/src/taproot.rs:449-534`; `packages/linker/src/taptree.rs:199-466`).

Deliverable 10, constructor reference graph — PARTIAL. The compact linker has typed nodes/edges, canonical SCCs, and cut policy, but they are `BundleSymbol`-specific; no STATE references exist and §9.7's Wave-4/Wave-6 split is unresolved (`packages/linker/src/graph.rs:35-53`; `packages/linker/src/graph.rs:254-378`; `packages/linker/src/graph.rs:411-537`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:1511-1528`).

The realization codec is independently named, big-endian, and 86 bytes while prototype metadata is little-endian and 48 bytes, and prototype program documentation refuses production conversion; the missing production constructor prevents a complete end-to-end non-leak test (`packages/realization/src/state_codec.rs:1-25`; `packages/realization/src/state_codec.rs:130-155`; `packages/target-elements-conformance/src/constructor/metadata.rs:24-40`; `packages/target-elements-conformance/src/constructor/metadata.rs:97-114`; `packages/target-elements-conformance/src/prototype_program.rs:1-36`).

The ground truth remains the six-field realization projection and `announce_maturity` transition over the canonical 86-byte codec, while §9.3's abstract oracle proves that the metadata leaf's abstract success and non-aborting-success sets are empty and its two target-native rows — no accepted spend and every attempted spend rejected — require target execution and remain outstanding under ruling 32 (`packages/model/src/pool.rs:11-19`; `packages/model/src/asset.rs:62-69`; `packages/realization/src/state.rs:20-70,228-271`; `packages/realization/src/state_codec.rs:1-158`; `packages/tapscript/src/stack.rs:157-229`; `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:1443-1466`).

The ten prototype-mechanism dispositions at (`rule:guide14-exec:prototype-promotion`) are now recorded: the dynamic metadata leaf, the always-aborting program, the static code subtree, the fixed branch side, deterministic nonce retry and the tweak-totality classification are promoted by re-implementation under rulings 22–25 and 28–29 at (`rule:phase6:wave4-rulings`); the public NUMS internal key is inherited with its residual under ruling 26; the synthetic counter field and the prototype widths and offsets are rejected as STATE semantics; and the prototype native report is historical evidence only.

The tree records two brief-versus-tree conflicts: the conceptual register does not assign `G14C-02` to Wave 4, because its row and coverage summary name Waves 2, 8, 9, 10 and 13 (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen_conceptual_register.md:83-86,118-129`); and no Wave-0 per-mechanism promotion disposition is recorded, because the guide carries only the initial disposition, the Phase-6 ground-truth record says the Wave-0 semantic census was omitted at (`sec:phase6:ground-truth`), and `T6-002` records a differently scoped review study (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:205-238`; `T6-002`).

The eight Wave-4 bites are codec field-slicing commutation (`T11-033`), constructor core (`T11-034`), oracle cross-checks (`T11-035`), constructor reference graph (`T11-036`), state-thread anchor (`T11-037`), membership seam (`T11-038`), writer retention (`T11-039`), and closure and handoff (`T11-040`); `T11-033`, `T11-038` and `T11-039` need no further decision and can start now, nothing is handed up because every decision was decidable on the merits, and the rulings are open to revision at the candidate audit like every ruling on this card.

The abstract oracle's reference had no independent contract for Boolean verification until the cross-check wrote one, and its first form omitted the target-domain abort that the production executor reports; the agreement test caught the omission, making this an oracle-coverage gain rather than a production defect (`0.6.178-dev`).

25. The anchor names the constructor generation by tapscript's type, making tapscript a direct dependency of transaction in the direction that already held through the linker; the manifests' boundary comments are repaired in this closure (`0.6.177-dev`).

Wave 4 closed in its planned eight-bite order: codec field-slicing commutation (`T11-033`, `0.6.172-dev`); fail-closed observed-operator membership (`T11-038`, `0.6.173-dev`); candidate STATE constructor (`T11-034`, `0.6.174-dev`); shared-writer request-subject retention (`T11-039`, `0.6.175-dev`); frozen STATE reference graph (`T11-036`, `0.6.176-dev`); typed state-thread anchor (`T11-037`, `0.6.177-dev`); oracle cross-checks (`T11-035`, `0.6.178-dev`); and closure and handoff (`T11-040`, `0.6.179-dev`). The untagged opening documentation established the ground truth, dispositions, rulings and eight-bite plan; the untagged realization README record documented the observed-operator-membership seam and its fail-closed boundary.

Deliverables 1–10 are met by the candidate STATE constructor in `0.6.174-dev`, with the codec commutation evidence in `0.6.172-dev` supporting deliverables 1 and 11 and the frozen graph in `0.6.176-dev` completing deliverable 10; deliverable 11 is met by the oracle cross-checks in `0.6.178-dev`. Exits 1, 2, 4 and 5 are met on the constructor and cross-check tests. Exit 3 is met on the abstract census alone, as ruling 32 states at (`rule:phase6:wave4-rulings`), while §9.3's two target-native rows remain recorded as outstanding for Wave 8.

Wave 5 produces the static subtree's production programs, and its first bite re-runs the Wave-4 derivation over them under ruling 24 and produces the membership witness under ruling 31 at (`rule:phase6:wave4-rulings`). Wave 6 owns cuts, binding-time resolution, the residual-DAG proof, canonical ordering and link integration over the frozen graph under ruling 28 at (`rule:phase6:wave4-rulings`) and the criterion at (`G14C-08`). Wave 7 carries the twelve field-slice commitments into observation and the ABI and binds the membership witness to finalized bytes under rulings 30 and 31 at (`rule:phase6:wave4-rulings`). Wave 8 owns the target-native metadata-leaf rows, the constructor carrier's ceremony and Rust test identity, and the live driver's capture schema under rulings 32 and 33 at (`rule:phase6:wave4-rulings`). Waves 9 and 10 attach accepted continuity and history evidence to the anchor under ruling 27 at (`rule:phase6:wave4-rulings`). Wave 12 owns the remaining resource criteria at (`G14C-03`).

The Wave-5 opening study was a read-only source study with no gate run, and every citation was self-audited.

The ground truth is 26 relations, 23 dependency edges and 15 public facts (`packages/realization/src/declarations/announce_maturity.rs:153-565`; `packages/realization/src/tests/announce_maturity_tests.rs:575-579`); the two external requirements are operator authorization and L-BTC substrate conservation (`packages/realization/src/evaluate.rs:53-104,371-386,623-629,673-695`); the exact witness graph requires `WitnessRole::OperatorAuthorization` at `AvailabilityClass::Operator` and admits optional sponsor-only `WitnessRole::SponsorAuthorization` at `AvailabilityClass::SponsorLocal` (`packages/realization/src/declarations/announce_maturity.rs:487-528`; `packages/realization/src/constructibility.rs:61-83`). The reviewed target has ten resource dimensions: consensus maxima are transaction weight 4,000,000, combined peak stack 1,000, stack element 520 bytes and control-path depth 128; validation budget, script bytes and operation cost are recorded unbounded; witness scale is 4, validation offset is 50 and policy transaction weight is 400,000; each signature or curve check charges 50 validation units, concatenation enforces the 520-byte ceiling, and tapscript adds a 10,000-instruction ceiling with abstract budgets of 4,096 states and 1,024 result alternatives (`packages/target-elements/src/resource.rs:29-68,231-258`; `packages/target-elements/src/opcode.rs:884-891,2122-2131`; `packages/tapscript/src/program.rs:59-66`; `packages/tapscript/src/stack.rs:241-280`). The fifteen §10 record fields are identity, semantic owner, prerequisites, typed fragment, abstract precondition, success states, non-aborting failures, abort causes, source requirements, witness role, constructibility, disclosure, resource projection, target evidence and residuals, with composed metadata derived from component records (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:1560-1578`). The whole-program final-stack rule requires a final canonical true instruction, every successful main stack exactly one item, no non-aborting failure, no alternate residue, no unknown-key unverified signature path and no resource defect (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:1724-1733`; `packages/tapscript/src/live_pattern.rs:1978-2033`).
