Relayability under a positive fee floor remains a deployment or Wave-12 question; root freshness remains with Wave 10, resource closure with Wave 12, and artifact promotion requires its own gate (`[ADR025-rem:relay-witness:consequences]`). `ConstructorContinuityObserved` and `NativeAcceptanceObserved` remain unpopulated in the current row classifier, because `T11-099` owns classification of the accepted bytes after the end-to-end continuity tests of `T11-097` (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_evidence.rs`).

The accepted comparison now supplies the evidence input for Wave 9's first exit: the accepted target bytes reconstruct both constructors and validate static and semantic continuity under the retained variable schedule, while the record of that exit and its no-inference judgement belongs to `T11-099` after `T11-097` at (`task:guide14-exec:wave9`). This route record closes its implementation and accepted-evidence dependency without minting those standings.

### Accepted-byte classification · `sec:phase6:accepted-byte-classification`

The classification study landed on `0.6.250-dev`: it settled D1–D9 and retained OQ1–OQ2 as matrix questions; `present_material_associates_exact_records_without_moving_standings` in `packages/vectors/src/maturity_evidence.rs` holds its one-row attribution premise, and `each_typed_non_answer_stands_on_exactly_the_rows_its_reason_names` in `packages/vectors/src/maturity_safety.rs` holds the positive rows' typed non-answers.

The projector's verified claim landed on `0.6.251-dev`: `accepted_archive_claim_survives_projection`, `a_claim_under_another_schedule_refuses`, `a_claim_whose_readback_bytes_are_not_the_submitted_bytes_refuses`, `a_claim_naming_another_identity_refuses`, `a_claim_whose_readback_witness_identity_is_wrong_refuses`, `an_outstanding_claim_leaves_every_projection_outstanding` and `the_published_identities_agree_with_the_accepted_readback` in `packages/vectors/src/maturity_continuity.rs` hold acceptance to the projected bytes, schedule and identities.

The report's source join landed on `0.6.253-dev`: `a_report_over_the_three_concrete_sources_establishes_acceptance`, `a_report_over_sources_without_a_claim_stays_outstanding`, `a_stated_acceptance_that_is_not_the_join_fails_validation` and `two_established_sources_that_disagree_refuse_assembly` in `packages/vectors/src/maturity_continuity_report.rs`, together with `accepted_archive_projection_and_report_establish_acceptance` in `packages/vectors/tests/maturity_continuity.rs`, hold the established, outstanding and disagreement cases.

The sponsorless standing and its declared environment premise landed on `0.6.252-dev`: `the_positive_row_names_the_accepted_run_and_its_disclosed_environment` and `an_outstanding_obligation_keeps_the_historical_standing` in `packages/vectors/src/maturity_evidence.rs` hold the row choice, while `admitted_archives_disclose_the_executor_fee_arguments` in `packages/vectors/src/maturity_corpus.rs` holds the premise's stated provenance.

D1. `ConstructorContinuityObserved` answers no row of the current matrix, and its zero bucket is a classification result. One standing stands per row; `present_material_associates_exact_records_without_moving_standings` attributes a validated report's entries to the sponsorless row alone, whose question is acceptance. The continuity mutants are experimental subjects and carry no countable row standing. The only §16.1 row naming constructor projection asks for semantic, constructor, root-history and public-recovery terms together; root-history and public-recovery have no producer that completes that row, and a continuity report alone cannot answer it under §1.14 at (`rule:guide14-exec:evidence-separation`), §23's separate conjuncts at (`gate:guide14-exec:exit`) and ruling 72 at (`rule:phase6:wave9-rulings`). `the_census_figures_are_unmoved_by_the_binding_readers` holds the bucket at zero; the standing remains part of the vocabulary without being assigned to a row.

D2. The sponsorless positive row takes `NativeAcceptanceObserved` in place of `NativeDeclaredBoundaryObserved`, answering the acceptance question named by §23's seventh conjunct at (`gate:guide14-exec:exit`) and assigned to `T11-111` at (`sec:phase6:wave9-findings`). `the_positive_row_names_the_accepted_run_and_its_disclosed_environment` holds the replacement, and `only_the_discharged_and_observed_standings_are_answered` holds that the new standing is answered just as the displaced standing was; the historical refusal remains in its admitted archive.

D3. The classifier takes acceptance from the admitted archive at `MATURITY_VARIABLE_RUN_ADDRESS`, rather than from caller-supplied constructor material or an arbitrary established report. `the_positive_row_names_the_accepted_run_and_its_disclosed_environment` holds the admitted source, and `present_material_associates_exact_records_without_moving_standings` holds that supplying material associates report records without changing row standings; this preserves the evidence boundary of ruling 73 at (`rule:phase6:wave9-rulings`).

D4. The sponsorless matrix row retains its relay-policy declaration, while the schedule-indexed `expected_layer` determines each archive's declared-boundary standing. `the_positive_row_names_the_accepted_run_and_its_disclosed_environment` holds the accepted branch and `an_outstanding_obligation_keeps_the_historical_standing` holds the historical fallback; the classifier reads the archive's `AnsweredAtDeclaredBoundary` result rather than duplicating that comparison.

D5. `NativeAcceptanceObserved` carries the run address, schedule, identity and block height, plus zero relay and block fee floors as a declared environment premise. `admitted_archives_disclose_the_executor_fee_arguments` holds `MaturityPremiseProvenance::ExecutorArguments` and the disclosed values; `the_positive_row_names_the_accepted_run_and_its_disclosed_environment` holds their passage into the standing. The immutable archive's readback binds transaction evidence, while its RUN-REPORT supplies no checked fee-floor field.

D6. The projector recomputes a stated acceptance claim against submitted bytes, stripped-encoding identity, complete-encoding witness identity and retained schedule. `accepted_archive_claim_survives_projection`, `a_claim_under_another_schedule_refuses`, `a_claim_whose_readback_bytes_are_not_the_submitted_bytes_refuses`, `a_claim_naming_another_identity_refuses` and `a_claim_whose_readback_witness_identity_is_wrong_refuses` hold those comparisons; `an_outstanding_claim_leaves_every_projection_outstanding` holds the absent or outstanding case.

D7. The report joins verified obligations across its sources: outstanding sources are silent, an established source supplies the result, and disagreeing established sources refuse assembly. `a_report_over_the_three_concrete_sources_establishes_acceptance`, `a_report_over_sources_without_a_claim_stays_outstanding`, `a_stated_acceptance_that_is_not_the_join_fails_validation` and `two_established_sources_that_disagree_refuse_assembly` hold the join and the validator's recomputed `AcceptanceDisposition`.

D8. Report completeness remains `PartialRequiredRowsOutstanding` because required native-run and report-layer rows remain outstanding even when accepted readback is present. `a_report_over_the_three_concrete_sources_establishes_acceptance` holds an established report, while `required_rows_remain_outstanding_after_the_admitted_run` holds the row obligation that keeps completeness partial.

D9. The established acceptance uses the renderer's existing lowercase vocabulary, including “acceptance established”, “acceptance_schedule” and “acceptance_identity”. `canonical_bytes_have_no_accepted_or_observed_standing_spelling` in `packages/vectors/src/maturity_continuity_report.rs` and `accepted_archive_projection_and_report_establish_acceptance` in `packages/vectors/tests/maturity_continuity.rs` hold the rendered disposition and spelling rule.

OQ1. Which of the ten other §16.1 acceptance-awaiting positive rows does the admitted route unblock for a case-specific target run? The §16 matrix owns the choice after the accepted route closed; `each_typed_non_answer_stands_on_exactly_the_rows_its_reason_names` holds the current typed reasons, including the sponsored cases whose separate arm remains scoped elsewhere.

OQ2. Does §16's last positive row state one four-term conjunction or four independently answerable rows? §16's text owns that matrix decision; `each_typed_non_answer_stands_on_exactly_the_rows_its_reason_names` holds its current acceptance-awaiting boundary, and `present_material_associates_exact_records_without_moving_standings` prevents a continuity report from silently dividing and answering it.

The recounted census has one `NativeAcceptanceObserved`, zero `NativeDeclaredBoundaryObserved`, 41 answered rows and a six-bucket partition of 40 + 43 + 42 + 1 + 15 + 65 = 206; the target-run register has 42 entries and is set-equal to its classified waiting rows. `the_census_figures_are_unmoved_by_the_binding_readers` and `the_census_buckets_are_the_figures_the_inputs_recompute` hold the census, while `every_outstanding_row_is_registered` and `the_register_length_is_the_figure_the_plan_recomputes` hold the register.

This record establishes acceptance only in the disclosed zero-fee-floor environment and establishes no relayability under a positive fee floor, root freshness, resource closure, artifact promotion or continuity standing on any row. Positive-fee-floor relayability belongs to deployment or Wave 12, root freshness to Wave 10 at (`task:guide14-exec:wave10`), resource closure to Wave 12, and artifact promotion to its §1.16 gate at (`rule:guide14-exec:candidate-only`); the continuity bucket remains zero under `the_census_figures_are_unmoved_by_the_binding_readers`.

Wave 10 receives the branch-context and root-freshness obligation from this classified accepted-byte record at (`task:guide14-exec:wave10`); Wave 12 receives positive-fee-floor relayability and resource closure, while OQ1 and OQ2 remain with the §16 matrix.

### Wave-10 plan findings · `sec:phase6:wave10-findings`

The Wave-10 opening study was a read-only source study with no gate run and self-audited citations; the citations below were located again at the tip this record opens from and its figures were recounted there. These totals are the wave's baseline, not evidence that the four planned producers have established their claims (`plans/drafts/wave10-root-history-and-recovery-study.md`).

154. Of §14.6's four named edge types, `OperationId` and `ProjectionId::TransitionCertificate` already exist, and the compiler requires that projection for `AnnounceMaturity`; the tree has `Outpoint` and a `CurrentStateOutpoint` view entry rather than a `StateOutpoint` type, while `StateRootEdge` and a certificate value for the report are absent. The architecture declares the transition projection and §3.1 assigns its subject to the architecture/model/realization boundary, so the missing value and edge are report work rather than fresh identifiers (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:717,2246-2261`, `packages/architecture/src/ids.rs:100-113,298-306`, `packages/architecture/src/spec.rs:649-655,1662`, `packages/compiler/src/maturity_announcement_plan.rs:57-61`, `packages/transaction/src/bytes.rs:541`, `packages/transaction/src/state_view.rs:84-92`).

155. §17.1 requires six ordered edge checks and exactly one member for one announcement; the landed projector already refuses extra inputs and outputs, the architecture fixes one STATE on each side, STATE-only succession and no deallocator, and `state_bundle_continuity` already implements §17.3's no-migration inequality. Those declarations and local refusals do not constitute the ordered edge validator: §14.6's invalid-intermediate case still needs a sequence refusal even if the final cursor matches (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:2246-2261,2716-2729,2750-2758`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_continuity.rs:595-604,1614`, `packages/architecture/src/spec.rs:619-622,1612-1647,2085-2093`, `packages/linker/src/state_bundle.rs:375-410`).

156. §14.7 lists eleven recovery-report items. Existing archive, transaction and witness producers supply paths to ten from public inputs, including the locator, exact bytes, output-0 program, decoded and derived metadata, nonce and reconstruction comparison; absence of creator-private inputs is the eleventh claim and requires the typed handoff's construction proof. The current `retained_context` path depends on the creator's retained instance and deployment identity, so it cannot serve as that public recovery path (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:2263-2279`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_native.rs:176-214`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_continuity.rs:181-189,247-251,591-621,637-694,811-851,1542-1590,1767-1775`, `packages/linker/src/state_bundle.rs:354-357,501-514`).

157. `classify` reads a row's refusing layer first and returns `OutstandingUnderTypedNonAnswer` when the row has none. Six typed reasons cover 65 rows in counts of 11, 12, 16, 11, 14 and 1; the sixteen §16.8 rows and thirteen §16.13 faults plus §16.1's `public-successor-recovery` make thirty rows under the two report reasons. `EvidenceBoundary` has thirteen members and no root-history or public-recovery boundary; the existing `RootHistoryObserved` and `PublicRecoveryObserved` standings both count zero. A report without the paired boundary therefore cannot change those rows' classification (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_evidence.rs:1197-1202,1269-1281,1443-1452,2065-2075,2831-2834`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_safety.rs:1460-1464,2351-2494,3227-3343,3806-3817`, `packages/vectors/src/matrix.rs:220-265`).

158. The recovery reason's statement that nothing in the tree publishes public bytes with a locator is falsified by the accepted archive: `MaturityAcceptedReadback` carries exact mined bytes, and `ArchivedSubmission` carries a run address. The narrower statement remains true: the tree has no typed public handoff and no process constrained to consume only it, so archived bytes alone do not establish §14.7 recovery (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_safety.rs:264-270,3219-3226`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_native.rs:176-214`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_continuity.rs:181-189`, `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:2263-2279`).

159. The root-history reason's broad claim that branch context cannot be projected, rewound and reprojected is falsified by the model's opaque `ModelIndexerCheckpoint` over a projected reference index and by the operator-right registry's typed reorganization path. The narrower statement remains true: these are modelled branch operations and caller-stated context; no layer here observes the current chain root or proves that an admitted transaction remains on a chosen chain prefix (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_safety.rs:249-255`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_evidence.rs:517-527`, `packages/model/src/ledger.rs:749-759,1790-1809`, `packages/transaction/src/operator_right.rs:23-58,432,510,795`).

160. §17.2 binds ten facts: network and genesis identities, block hash and height, transaction identity, predecessor and successor outpoints, and target contract are carried or derived from the admitted archive and public bytes; the linked candidate and candidate ABI are declared premises. `MaturityPremiseProvenance` already distinguishes a declared fee-floor premise from a checked archive binding, giving the checkpoint a local form for those two declarations. A modelled reorganization can stale the binding without becoming a chain observation (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:2731-2748`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_corpus.rs:138-155,395-412`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_native.rs:176-214`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_continuity.rs:1587-1590,1745`, `packages/model/src/ledger.rs:1790-1809`).

161. The pinned baseline is 206 matrix rows and 41 answered, with six partition buckets of 40 first-party discharged, 43 first-party required, 42 native-run required, 1 native acceptance observed, 15 report-layer required and 65 typed non-answers; the root-history and public-recovery observed counters are zero, and the waiting register has 42 entries. The thirty retyped rows described by the study would make those two counters 16 and 14, answered 71 and typed non-answers 35, with the surviving reason counts 11, 12, 11 and 1 and eight partition buckets still summing to 206. These latter values are bite gates, not opening-tree standings (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_evidence.rs:2784-2848,3340-3363`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_safety.rs:3806-3817`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_negative_half.rs:680-700,773-784`).

162. The study's four bite exits are expected as follows: one valid accepted-archive STATE edge recorded is MET by bites 1 and 4, while §23's stronger independently recovered edge is CONDITIONALLY MET on the disclosed environment; refusal of an invalid intermediate edge despite final-cursor equality is MET by bite 1; output-0 reconstruction by a process constrained through the typed handoff and import list is CONDITIONALLY MET by bite 3; and exclusion of creator-private state is MET for recovery through four construction proofs but NOT MET for root history, whose linked-candidate and candidate-ABI bindings remain declared premises. These are expected post-bite dispositions, not opening-tree evidence (`plans/drafts/wave10-root-history-and-recovery-study.md`, `plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:2246-2279,2716-2748`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_corpus.rs:138-155`).

The obligations have owners: `T11-114` through `T11-117` carry Wave 10's edge, checkpoint, handoff and report gates; the §16 matrix owns the accepted-byte study's OQ1 and OQ2 and this study's two candidate-audit readings; deployment or Wave 12 owns positive-fee-floor relayability, Wave 12 owns resource closure, and artifact promotion retains its §1.16 gate at (`rule:guide14-exec:candidate-only`). The register's shared clauses remain subject to their other owning waves.

This record opens Wave 10 with ruled decisions, provisional readings, rows and a relocated baseline. It establishes no typed edge, public recovery, chain observation, row standing, census change or candidate adoption; the accepted archive supplies bytes and a disclosed environment, not those producer verdicts.

The four bites receive the thirty boundaryless rows and the separate evidence roles at (`task:guide14-exec:wave10`); `T11-118` receives the evidence-based exit, register and `R-5 RECOVERY` dispositions. The §16 matrix receives the unresolved positive-row and cursor interpretations, while deployment or Wave 12 receives the fee-floor and resource obligations and §1.16 retains artifact promotion.

The Wave-10 closure is a precisely partial stopping record under `T11-118`. Its findings and figures were located again at `0.6.265-dev`, the tip it is written against, with one qualification: this record deletes the commented-out pattern line that stood at `packages/vectors/src/maturity_safety.rs:1053`, so every anchor it cites into that file below that line is read after the deletion. Its evidence is the tags and the tests that pin each claim, read rather than run.

163. The root-history registry landed on `0.6.259-dev` as `MaturityMutationRegistry`, not under the outstanding-named type that ruling 83, finding 150 and row `T11-115` named, and its `registered` constructor carries no visibility modifier, so it is private to `packages/vectors/src/maturity_evidence.rs` rather than crate-private as ruling 83 stated (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_evidence.rs:613-661`, `registered` at `:622`); its kind enum has the one member `RootHistory` (`:524-545`), because `T11-096` replaced the constructor registry with typed constructor material on `0.6.238-dev`. A type named for a standing its own census computes would state a standing the census can contradict, and a module-private constructor is reachable only from the derivation beside it, which is stronger than the crate-private path the ruling asked for. Ruling 83 and finding 150 are retyped in place to the tree's name, finding 150 in the past tense of the tip it recorded, and the row states the landed name.

164. The root-history report reads neither declared premise's provenance. Its renderer writes the constant lines `checkpoint_linked_candidate declared deployment-declaration` and `checkpoint_candidate_abi declared deployment-declaration` (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_history_report.rs:586-593`), none of its nineteen recomputed items compares a premise (`:116-136`), and `MaturityRootCheckpoint::bind` stores either member of the two-member `MaturityPremiseProvenance` without checking it (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_history.rs:596`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_corpus.rs:140-145`). A report over a checkpoint whose premises were declared from executor arguments therefore validates and renders byte-identically to one whose premises carry the deployment declaration. A later code bite either adds a recomputed item comparing both carried provenances with `DeploymentDeclaration` or renders the carried provenance; this record adds neither.

165. The evidence derivation's checkpoint declares a linked candidate and a candidate ABI that did not produce the accepted bytes, a defect for a later code bite. The derivation links the plan's bundle under the retained whole-metadata selection and derives its ABI over that bundle's view (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_evidence.rs:1987-1995`), and declares both as `DeploymentDeclaration` premises of the checkpoint (`:2014-2026`), while the edge's continuity is projected from the replayed planner's bundle under the retained variable-metadata selection (`:1885-1890`, `:1948`). `binds` compares the transaction identity, the successor and the predecessor and neither premise (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_history.rs:644`), so the history report validates over premises that are not the accepted transaction's; `the_checkpoint_binds_eight_facts_and_declares_two` already pins the ABI premise's schedule as different from the continuity's while the checkpoint binds (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_history.rs:1124-1128`). The later bite declares the replayed planner's bundle and its ABI as the premises, or compares the declared bundle with the continuity's before the report is assembled.

166. The root-history edge is projected through the creator's retained context, so no edge is yet built from the public handoff alone. `StateRootEdge::from_continuity` takes a validated continuity (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_history.rs:114`), the projection input that validates it carries the retained bundle and the caller's deployment identity (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_continuity.rs:205-217`), `retained_context` refuses unless the bundle's first retained instance and that identity agree with the witnessed bytes (`:680-690`), and the derivation's replay rebuilds the planner from the corpus's deployment identity (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_evidence.rs:1883-1890`). The successor program is independently reconstructed from the handoff on `0.6.261-dev`, and the edge's two outpoints are public-byte facts the checkpoint verifies against the edge and the funded outpoint, but the edge value and its certificate are recomputed from the admitted bytes through that creator-side projection. §23's eleventh conjunct at (`gate:guide14-exec:exit`) is therefore CONDITIONALLY MET on this condition beside the disclosed environment, and the fourth exit's root-history NOT MET has it as a ground; an edge built from the public handoff alone would discharge the condition.

167. The four reports spell the target contract revision three ways. The continuity and safety reports write `target_contract {:?}` and `execution_domain {:?}` in Debug case (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_continuity_report.rs:2141-2142`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_report.rs:1329-1330`), the root-history report writes the same two Debug lines and then ASCII-lowercases its whole finished text (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_history_report.rs:512-513`, `:616`), and the public-recovery report writes `contract_revision` from `TargetContractVersion::get` with no execution-domain line (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_recovery_report.rs:767`). No `name()` accessor exists for the two older headers to adopt: `TargetContractVersion` carries `get()` alone (`packages/target-elements/src/definition.rs:124-128`), and `ExecutionDomain` carries only the census `ALL` its macro generates (`packages/target-elements/src/opcode.rs:32-44`, `packages/target-elements/src/capability.rs:47-77`). Each report validates and reproduces its own bytes, and `no_role_spelling_crosses_between_the_reports` compares role and residual lines rather than headers, so the difference is recorded as the reports' design as landed; aligning the spellings changes the older reports' canonical bytes and first needs an accessor neither type carries.

168. The evidence derivation refuses rather than leaving rows outstanding when the accepted archive does not replay or a report refuses. `derive_maturity_evidence_plan_with` replays the accepted archive through a staged, fallible replay, binds the checkpoint and assembles and validates both reports before it classifies any row (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_evidence.rs:2011-2056`), and each refusal leaves through its own member: `AcceptedReplayRefused` over the eight `MaturityAcceptedReplayStage` members, `AcceptedContinuityRefused`, `RootHistoryCheckpointRefused`, `RootHistoryReportRefused`, `PublicRecoveryReportRefused`, and `RowAbsentFromItsReport` for a row declaring a report layer its validated report does not observe (`:1624-1691`, `:1706-1739`, `:2334-2355`). `MaturityEvidenceRefusal` moved from fourteen members to twenty, one more than the five the study's bite-4c cut named, because the replay maps every planner, step, submission and funding refusal to a typed stage rather than an `expect`; `FundedCoinUnreadable` carries the failed `MaturityFundedCoinField` and the offending wire text, because `outpoint_of`, `asset_of` and `decode_hex` return `Option` and so hand back no refusal to carry (`:1695-1702`, `:1846-1876`, `packages/vectors/src/live_owner_observation.rs:1182-1212`). The reports are built from pinned admitted bytes, so a refusal there is a defect rather than a waiting state, and a derivation that refuses publishes no figure a reader could take as answered; row `T11-117` now states this in place of its outstanding-rows sentence.

169. `G14C-13`'s explicit non-claims are not carried by the public-recovery report at the stopping record. Its four residuals state no chain observation, no root-cursor freshness, no archive origin beyond admission and no leastness of the witnessed nonce (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_recovery_report.rs:60-82`), while the row's criterion also asks for five non-claims: independent implementation, trust anchor, inclusion proof, canonical chain authentication and static-subtree data availability (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen_conceptual_register.md`). The last is concrete here: the handoff's static subtree is rebuilt from witness item seven because the linked taptree is the one-leaf singleton finding 148 records (`packages/vectors/src/maturity_recovery_report.rs:861-890`), so a subtree carrying support leaves would enter the handoff as a published parameter whose availability no report item or residual states. On `0.6.270-dev`, the five non-claims became typed residuals in the report's validated bytes, and `G14C-13` closes on their evidence pointer below; `R-5 RECOVERY`, which already defers trust anchors, inclusion proofs and implementation diversity, remains adopted on the reconstruction that landed.

170. `G14C-11` and Wave 10's clause of `G14C-12` are advanced only by a modelled prefix. The third exit — an unrelated process reconstructs output 0 — is CONDITIONALLY MET. `the_accepted_archives_handoff_recovers_output_zero_from_public_values` on `0.6.261-dev` recovers the accepted archive's output-0 program from a handoff the binary builds of published values, `the_accepted_archives_public_recovery_report_validates_from_the_handoff_alone` on `0.6.264-dev` assembles, validates and renders the report from that handoff and finds the rendered `programs_agree true` line, and `assembly_takes_the_handoff_alone` binds the report's producer to the one-handoff function type (`packages/vectors/tests/maturity_recovery.rs`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_recovery_report.rs`). The condition is that the process is constrained by a function type and a pinned list of twenty-nine imports, asserted by `the_binarys_import_list_excludes_the_bundle_the_identity_and_both_planners`, rather than by a crate boundary, so the proofs show what the process names, not that no path to creator-private state exists. §23's thirteenth conjunct, reconstruction from public chain data, holds under the same condition and one more: the bytes are the admitted archive's mined readback, captured once in the disclosed environment and not observed by the process.

The fourth exit — no creator-private state is used — is MET for the public-recovery path and CONDITIONALLY MET for the root-history path. The four construction proofs in `packages/vectors/src/maturity_recovery.rs` and the binary's import-list test hold the recovery claim with the third exit's condition. On `0.6.268-dev`, the checkpoint's two premises are the bundle and ABI under which the accepted bytes reproduce against the handoff's published parameters, and the history report checks and renders their carried provenance; on `0.6.271-dev`, the edge is built from the public handoff alone. The root-history condition is the declared linked candidate carried whole, retained instance included: §17.2 binds it as a premise, no verdict reads that instance, and `plans/drafts/wave10-register-closure-study.md`'s OQ2 ("Whether Wave 10's fourth exit governs the root-history path") owns that interpretation. The Phase-6 gate at (`gate:guide14-exec:exit`) reads that condition under its eleventh conjunct.

The register exit is PASSED. `G14C-11` closes on the modelled branch realizations, suffix invalidation and reproduction of `0.6.272-dev` and the branch-bound token and fence of `0.6.273-dev`; Wave 10's clause of `G14C-12` closes on the class-two transitions and standings of `0.6.274-dev`; and `G14C-13` closes on the five typed non-claims of `0.6.270-dev`: independent implementation, trust anchor, inclusion proof, canonical chain authentication and static-subtree data availability. Their pinning tests and modelled-evidence qualifications are in the three register cells. `G14C-02` retains the history and recovery reports as validated witnesses of one accepted step and remains OPEN for its other owning waves. `R-5 RECOVERY` remains ADOPTED with its condition: reconstruction from the public handoff rests on published deployment parameters and a producer constrained by a function type and pinned import list, with no chain observed. With `T11-119` through `T11-126` disposed, Wave 10 has no open owned task row; the register recounts five OPEN and ten CLOSED rows over 26 wave assignments (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen_conceptual_register.md`).

The remaining obligations have owners. Chain observation, root-cursor freshness and archive origin beyond admission stand as typed residuals in both reports' validated bytes, and the fourth exit's declared linked candidate carried whole stands under OQ2 of `plans/drafts/wave10-register-closure-study.md`, all read by the Phase-6 gate at (`gate:guide14-exec:exit`); the eleven acceptance-awaiting positive rows at this Wave-10 baseline, §16.1's four-term row among them, stand beside the post-`T11-132` split of nine acceptance-awaiting, eleven construction-refused and three region-scoping typed non-answers, with the positive rows' separate question staying with the §16 matrix under the accepted-byte study's OQ1 and OQ2 at (`sec:phase6:accepted-byte-classification`); positive-fee-floor relayability belongs to deployment or Wave 12, resource closure to Wave 12, and artifact promotion to its §1.16 gate at (`rule:guide14-exec:candidate-only`); finding 167's report-header spellings stay as landed.

This record disposes Wave 10's task rows on their landed evidence and states its four exits: the first and second MET, the third CONDITIONALLY MET, and the fourth MET for public recovery and CONDITIONALLY MET for root history, with §23's eleventh conjunct conditionally met only on the disclosed zero-fee-floor environment, the thirteenth conditionally met under its stated conditions and the twelfth met. This record disposes `T11-119` through `T11-126` on their landed evidence, closes `G14C-11`, `G14C-13` and Wave 10's clause of `G14C-12`, and passes the register exit while treating neither the modelled branch as a chain view nor the public-byte reconstruction as a chain or origin proof.

### Wave-11 plan findings · `sec:phase6:wave11-findings`

The Wave-11 opening study was a read-only source study with no gate run and self-audited citations; the citations below are located again at the tip this record opens from and its figures are recounted there. These totals are the wave's baseline, not evidence that its four planned bites have established their claims (`plans/drafts/wave11-sponsored-forms-study.md`).

180. The request type admits four forms. Sponsorless without change has `NativeAcceptanceObserved` on the admitted archive's `sponsorless` row; sponsorless with change is refused by `MaturityAnnouncementRequest::new` as `SponsorChangeWithoutSponsoredForm`; sponsored without change is pinned to `SponsoredMaturityFormHasNoCarrier` at construction step 1; sponsored with change reaches that same construction refusal, while its existing test only constructs and reads back the request. Thus the fourth construction disposition is unpinned at this baseline and belongs to `T11-129` (`plans/phases/06-state-and-maturity.md:984`, `packages/transaction/src/state_request.rs:248-262`, `packages/transaction/src/state_construct.rs:208-215`, `packages/transaction/src/tests/state_request_tests.rs:63-78,100-112`, `packages/transaction/src/tests/state_construct_tests.rs:303-319`).

181. Of Wave 11's nine deliverables, the sponsorless control has an admitted node acceptance in the disclosed zero-fee-floor environment; sponsored control, change absent and change present have host refusals but need C1 and a node for acceptance; missing authorization has a model refusal and needs C1 for host completion and C2 for its target verdict; STATE/sponsor overlap needs C1 for its host ABI refusal; balanced STATE corruption has a model reading and needs C1 and C2 for its target half; report exclusion has a host structural reading and `T11-131`'s key tests; exact target and policy verdicts have only the sponsorless node subject. The sponsored node subjects also require an Elements-provisioned compute node (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:3149-3172`, `plans/phases/06-state-and-maturity.md:994`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_native.rs:57-70`, `packages/realization/src/tests/open_flow_tests.rs:333-345`, `packages/transaction/src/state_construct.rs:201-215`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_safety.rs:2649-2782`).

182. §16.10 has fourteen sponsor faults in guide order. Twelve mutate `Control::SponsoredAnnouncement` and stand as typed non-answers: nine—`state-sponsor-overlap`, `foreign-sponsor-asset`, `missing-sponsor-authorization`, `sponsor-change-at-state-output-0`, `state-successor-in-sponsor-range`, `fee-change-substitution`, `empty-sponsor-offer-for-sponsored-request`, `balanced-state-corruption-compensated-by-sponsor-change`, and `confidential-sponsor-value-where-the-selected-target-policy-claims-support`—reach ABI, completion, target or selected-policy boundaries that the refit does not alter once a sponsored control exists. Three—`two-sponsor-envelopes`, `sponsor-member-unclassified`, and `zero-valued-sponsor-member-under-exact-role-structure`—ask about multiplicity or membership that `T11-064` re-scopes or retires. The other two, `report-publishes-sponsor-amount` and `report-publishes-sponsor-opening`, have a canonical-report control and stand `ReportLayerRequired`. Its sponsored observation has one sponsor input and output, a fee-sponsor flow and signer; the model accepts isolation with that signer and refuses a missing signer, a protocol amount on a sponsor member, and an unclaimed member. `ObservedValue::SponsorOpaque` and the announcement's expression, disclosure and constructibility graphs expose no sponsor amount fact. `T11-130` compares the other 20 identities and the same STATE corruption on both observations (`packages/realization/src/declarations/announce_maturity.rs:60-82,185-204,228-276,315-328`, `packages/realization/src/tests/open_flow_tests.rs:50-59,309-383`, `packages/realization/src/tests/announce_maturity_tests.rs:1014-1050`).

### Wave-12 plan findings · `sec:phase6:wave12-findings`

The Wave-12 study was a read-only source study with no run; the citations below are located again at the opening tree and their figures are recounted there. These findings state the baseline the five bites move from, not evidence that their planned tests or reports have passed (`plans/drafts/wave12-resource-study.md`).

204. The leaf measurement has six measured dimensions and five absent ones, rather than the five-and-five wording in Wave 6's handoff: `measure_state_totals` inserts `ScriptBytes`, `OperationCost`, `ValidationBudget`, `InitialStackItems`, `PeakStackItems` and `InitialWitnessItemBytes`, and `the_checked_totals_are_the_linked_program_s_own` asserts six totals and five absences. The sixth entered with `T11-103` on `0.6.239-dev` and `T11-104` on `0.6.240-dev`; the handoff's earlier sentence remains as a historical reading, while this record uses six and five (`packages/linker/src/state_resource.rs:325–348`, `packages/linker/src/state_resource.rs:406–418`, `packages/linker/src/tests/state_resource_tests.rs:207–223`, `plans/phases/06-state-and-maturity.md:625`, `plans/phases/06-state-and-maturity.md:657`, `plans/backlog.md:1296–1297`).

The whole archive pins 1,694 candidate bytes, 130 stripped bytes and host weight matching its node response; the variable capture records 1,701 candidate bytes, whose same 130 stripped bytes give the variable host weight by the study's arithmetic, 3 × 130 + 1,701, a first reading for `T11-136` to pin. The variable node weight is read from the capture payload, while the per-operation capture lines contain no resource field; `ValidatedMaturityCorpus::exchanges` admits the response payload, and no maturity module compares either observation with a host prediction (`packages/vectors/fixtures/maturity-run-of-record/`, `packages/vectors/fixtures/maturity-variable-run-of-record/`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_continuity.rs:3649–3652`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_corpus.rs:609`, `packages/vectors/src/maturity_corpus.rs:1356–1371`, `packages/vectors/tests/common/mod.rs:1080–1143`).

(d) Wave 0's remaining deliverable, the §5.1 carrier-sufficiency proof, was not recorded. §5.1 accepts the witness carrier only if Wave 0 proves that an unrelated process can reconstruct the successor from the accepted transaction alone, and directs that implementation stop for a focused carrier decision if that proof fails. The proof is now recorded at (`sec:phase6:carrier-proof`), and this item is discharged.

(f) Public STATE facts are side-indexed input/output keys. Ruling 13 assigns `FactId::StateField` the operation, transaction side, and field key and opens the combined refit in `T11-021`; this item is discharged.

### Wave-0 carrier sufficiency · `sec:phase6:carrier-proof`

The claim proved here is recovery, not fresh consensus validation: Guide 14 makes the accepted announcement transaction's public script-path witness and output set the publication source (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:270-283`), and §5.1 says the witness publishes predecessor-construction data, the announced cycle, and the canonical successor representation nonce without a second metadata output (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:882-890`).

The carrier's public recovery inputs divide between facts already owned by this tree and exact construction work a later wave still owes:

| Recovery value from §1.8 | Public source | Owned today | Later wave owes |
|---|---|---|---|
| predecessor metadata | STATE input witness, in full | `StateMetadata` owns the six-field census `omega`, `y_l`, `y_t`, `q`, `cycle`, and `maturity` (`packages/realization/src/state.rs:44-70`). | The Wave-1 codec bite must decode all six fields under the published schema rather than substitute a digest. |
| requested announcement cycle | STATE input witness | The ordinal `Cycle` domain and checked advance are typed at `packages/realization/src/domain.rs:134-168`; `announce_maturity` accepts the announced `Cycle` at `packages/realization/src/state.rs:223-227`. | The codec must give that cycle one canonical public representation. |
| successor semantic metadata | derived from the preceding two values, not separately published | The total transition and its closed result are owned at `packages/realization/src/state.rs:210-252`. | Model conformance must continue to pin the projection law named by the second Wave-1 ruling (`plans/phases/06-state-and-maturity.md:142`). |
| successor representation nonce | STATE input witness | The fifth Wave-1 ruling fixes big-endian canonical metadata encoding and a `u32` nonce (`plans/phases/06-state-and-maturity.md:148`); semantic STATE deliberately excludes representation (`packages/realization/src/state.rs:9-15`). | The in-flight Wave-1 codec bite must implement those ruled bytes, and the constructor wave must verify the canonical retry result. |
| metadata schema | published recovery schema | The semantic field set and the ruled byte order and nonce width are fixed by the two owners above. | The codec bite must finish the exact field layout, discriminants, versioning, and strict decoding. |
| static constructor recipe or exact linked static-root reference | STATE input witness and published constructor schema | The successor must reuse one exact linked static subtree, with a dynamic metadata leaf and fixed branch side (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:892-918`); the leading construction combines that root with the metadata leaf (`plans/research/state-constructor.md:137-175`). | The constructor wave must implement and bind that recipe or reference without an additional private input. |
| successor output position | accepted transaction's public output set | §1.8 requires the position as recovery data (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:274-283`). | The transaction and ABI waves must bind its canonical representation and validate the selected output. |
| target leaf version and internal-key policy | witness-visible target policy and published constructor schema | The research requires target leaf-version capability (`plans/research/state-constructor.md:60-72`) and a deterministic, publicly auditable internal key (`plans/research/state-constructor.md:177-193`). | The target and constructor waves must bind the exact leaf version and key while preserving those policies. |

As implemented, `announce_maturity` is a total deterministic function of predecessor metadata, announced cycle, and typed lead bounds: every input returns either one complete successor or one member of the closed refusal sum (`packages/realization/src/state.rs:155-208`, `:210-252`).

For an accepted announcement, the successful branch is unique: it copies `omega`, `y_l`, `y_t`, `q`, and the predecessor's current `cycle`, and replaces only `maturity` with `Announced { cycle: announced_cycle }` (`packages/realization/src/state.rs:72-89`, `:238-252`).

Thus each of the six successor fields has a public source: `omega`, `y_l`, `y_t`, `q`, and `cycle` come from the full predecessor metadata, while `maturity` comes from the announced cycle plus the publicly visible fact that the transaction was accepted.

Once the ruled encoding is implemented, successor metadata bytes are the deterministic encoding of that unique semantic successor and the public successor nonce; neither value admits an additional author.

Under §5.2 through §5.4, constructor bytes are then a deterministic function of those metadata bytes, the shared static subtree, the fixed branch side, deterministic nonce retry, and the published target policy (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:892-946`).

Recovery steps 1 and 2 locate the accepted transaction and verify its bytes and deployment binding; both operate on the named public publication source (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:272-283`, `:285-289`).

Step 3 decodes the witness under the published schema, and step 4 applies the typed transition to the recovered predecessor metadata and announced cycle (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:289-290`).

Step 5 evaluates the canonical constructor from the encoded successor, shared static subtree, and target policy, and step 6 compares the result with the output at the carried public position (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:291-292`).

The lead bounds are not a successor-derivation input after acceptance: they decide whether the transition may succeed, but the accepted successor itself is the field-for-field mapping above.

Those bounds currently live as `min_maturity_lead` and `max_maturity_lead` in the model's `Constants` (`packages/model/src/constants.rs:17-18`), while the realization transition receives a validated typed pair (`packages/realization/src/state.rs:92-152`, `:223-227`).

A third party independently checking validity therefore also needs the consensus constants published elsewhere; whether the typed architecture must name them remains open in question (b) above (`plans/phases/06-state-and-maturity.md:162`).

The predecessor's own representation nonce is likewise unnecessary for successor derivation: it authenticates predecessor constructor linkage, whereas the successor constructor consumes its separately public successor nonce (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:240-258`).

The proof holds only while the witness carries predecessor metadata in full, rather than a hash of it, and carries the announced cycle; otherwise the reconstructor cannot recover the five copied fields or the new `maturity` value.

It would also fail if `omega`, `y_l`, `y_t`, `q`, or `cycle` acquired any source other than predecessor metadata, if `maturity` acquired any source other than predecessor maturity plus the announced cycle, or if encoding or construction consulted data outside the witness, output set, and published schemas.

Accordingly, the §5.1 carrier is accepted for the initial candidate, no second publication is introduced, and the residual obligation is a constructor-wave test tripwire proving that the constructor remains a function of exactly the public inputs listed here.

### Declaration prerequisites · `sec:phase6:declaration-prerequisites`

Three closed-vocabulary gaps precede an honest announce-maturity declaration:

| Gap | Tree evidence | Vocabulary owed before declaration |
|---|---|---|
| operator relation | Only owner and permissionless bodies exist (`packages/realization/src/relation.rs:42-68`); STATE has no owner, permissionlessness requires no signer, and the model requires the operator (`packages/realization/src/evaluate.rs:371-374,828-840`; `packages/model/src/ops/maturity.rs:34-40`). | Add evidence-required `OperatorAuthorization`, then cover the exhaustive sites at `packages/compiler/src/requirement.rs:541-550`, `source.rs:230-237`, `layout.rs:442-451`, `placement.rs:566-573`, `coverage.rs:763-780`, and `packages/realization/src/tests/compact_ash_tests.rs:1695-1704`. |
| operator constructibility | Only `PublicPermissionless` and `OwnersOf` exist (`packages/realization/src/relation.rs:20-25`), while operator graph support already exists (`packages/realization/src/constructibility.rs:34-43,276-289`; `packages/realization/src/validate.rs:493`). | Add `ConstructibilityClass::Operator`; reuse the existing graph authorization. |
| public STATE fields | `FactId` lacks STATE-field keys and `FamilyAmount` is aggregate (`packages/realization/src/identity.rs:16-77`); `StateMetadata` exposes six fields (`packages/realization/src/state.rs:44-70`). | Add typed input/output identities for all six, without amount or boolean surrogates. |

The dependency split is vocabulary, then declaration, then executed-model conformance. The declaration bite owns the exact relation/lifecycle censuses, scoped mirror, content pins and corruption tests; its mirror adds only `StateInput` and `StateOutput` to the current mismatch enum (`packages/realization/src/error.rs:12-37`) because generic cardinality and recognition stay in `packages/realization/src/validate.rs:318-375`.

The declaration sits at the Wave-1/Wave-2 boundary: it closes Wave 1's realization semantics and supplies Wave 2's declaration-derived exact relation census (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:2873-2892`, `:2900-2917`). Wave 3 still owns operator-key encoding, message recomputation and target-native signature control (`plans/guides/guide_fourteen.md:2925-2935`), separated by ruling 6's external requirement.

The three declaration-prerequisite gaps are closed by the vocabulary landing in `0.6.147-dev`; the announce-maturity declaration, its dependency-release follow-up, and its executed-model conformance landed in `0.6.148-dev`.

## Exit gate · `gate:phase6:exit`

Phase 6 exits when:

- accepted constructor research is implemented exactly;
- metadata encoding is canonical;
- predecessor and successor share one authenticated static code identity;
- no key-path or metadata path bypass remains;
- announce-maturity matches the model relation;
- all wrong-code-subtree vectors reject;
- root-history continuity tests pass;
- target and policy resources fit;
- bundle/ABI/report identities are deterministic;
- relation coverage is complete and the repository remains clean.
